Dance Annex — Message Plumbing Map

Every inbound message path, what moves it, and whether it survives a Claude token outage. Click any node for detail. Compiled 2026-08-11 from RUNTIMES.md and the event-gate source in dc-canon — maintained since; see the change log at the foot of the page.

The whole network — six lines · filled stops are the bots

bot — does the work, cadence under its name system — passed through you / staff backup — not relied on

The fleet — who does the work, what it files, who checks it · times America/Toronto

The lines — what’s built, and which bot runs it · click a stop

machine you closed route the card — every line meets bold mono = the runtime that drives that stop

The booking line — who runs which stop · click a stop

machine you sends dispatched by the send engine

🔴 Broken / not yet trusted — fix in this order

  1. RISKRotate mcp-auth-token — it reached a chat transcript.
    Three touchpoints; miss one and every tick 401s silently

    The secret itself, the alarm-mcp redeploy, and both Cloud Scheduler job headers must change together.

  2. GAPNo emergency lane watches email.
    An email-borne emergency waits for the next LLM pass

    SMS has the deterministic 1-min regex lane; email has nothing. Worst case ~2h daytime, ~10h across the overnight Concierge gap. Open since the map was compiled.

  3. WATCHAlarm watchdog is ARMED — and it has already acted wrongly once.
    Re-armed 2026-08-22 behind the occupancy guard; WATCHDOG_ARMED=true, rev alarm-mcp-00053-2rk

    It armed a studio on a person. Studio 693, 2026-08-22, 12:20–12:25 Toronto. Hanano was inside. An alarm fired, she disarmed at 12:20, and the watchdog disarmed and armed away on her at 12:22 and again at 12:24 — she had to disarm three times in five minutes. The same defect hit Sara in 509B the same day. Root cause: in_alarm stays LATCHED TRUE after a human disarm, and executor.py’s pre-command recheck read in_alarm and nothing else — so a person dealing with her own alarm read as “siren still going, nobody has dealt with it”.

    The fix is the occupancy guard — _safe_to_cycle(), every reason NOT to act, checked before any command and never between a disarm and a re-arm. Three questions in order: (1) is the partition still armed — asked first and independently of in_alarm, because somebody disarming is the clearest presence signal this system will ever get; (2) is it still in alarm; (3) is any door open or any motion sensor live. Re-armed the same day behind it.

    State, read live 2026-08-26 03:24Z rather than recalled: flag set on alarm-mcp-00053-2rk, and the panel is quiet — five partitions armed_away, nothing faulted, nothing in_alarm, zero bypassed sensors. Ledgers persist to gs://danceannex-alarm-watchdog, so a cold start cannot forget a suppression.

    Why it still says WATCH. The guard is four days old and has not yet been tested by a real trip with somebody in the room — which is precisely the case it exists for. Kill switch, no redeploy: gcloud run services update alarm-mcp --region us-central1 --remove-env-vars WATCHDOG_ARMED.

  4. DECIDEBypass on a disarmed studio — hold or arm-to-clear?
    Policy call, not code

    Clearing means arming, and a disarmed studio may have someone inside. Junyan's call.

  5. TRICKLELegacy alarm windows migrate one at a time.
    No big-bang panel edit

    Windowed users surface in the Doorman's 7-day table as legacy-window; each is a manual delete → 10-min sync → re-add-as-permanent.

The pipes — what happens without you

Six flows, left to right. Click any hop for its full detail (panel at the foot). Green hops keep running in a token outage; amber hops stop; violet is you.

1 · A renter texts SMS · survives outage until the reply step
Phone.com SMS→ event-gate 1-min poll→ emergency?🚨 on-call SMS+Staff Call Queue simple question?quick-answer card everything elseResponder 30-min→held draft · you release→send engine

Without you: emergencies summon staff and simple questions get answered even in a full token outage. A judgment reply is drafted within ~30 min but nothing sends until a human releases it.

2 · An email arrives business Gmail · the weakest inbound pipe
Business Gmail→ gmail_pull ledger→ Concierge 2-hourly triage→ Notion tickets

Without you: mail is archived deterministically, but nothing acts on it until an LLM pass — up to ~2h daytime, ~10h overnight. No emergency regex watches this pipe (fix-list item 3). The ledger cron rides flaky GitHub Actions.

3 · A message goes out every outbound email/SMS · human-gated by design
agent drafts a queue row→ Pending Review · you release→ send engine 1-min poll · v18 guards→ renter

Without you: nothing customer-facing sends — your release is the valve. The engine refuses malformed rows instead of sending them (single sender identity, Invariant J unthreaded-reply guard); failures flip to Error + alert email, never silent. Scheduled FBS sends (HTA/EOB/AVA) flow once queued.

4 · A booking becomes access Skedda → alarm codes · ⚠ upstream data is broken
Skedda ⚠ cookie→ fbs-monitor 15-min rebuild→ Doorman 04:00 + ~06:00→ PIN digits — staff→ HTA with code

Without you: the board rebuilds and the Doorman preflights access 7 days out, checks arm state overnight, and proposes (never executes) code retirements. PIN digits stay human — the panel masks them. Everything here reads through the broken Skedda cookie (fix-list item 1) until it is reseeded.

5 · Something breaks watchers watching workers · deterministic where it counts
fbs-watch 15-min→ Slack #desk-status Sentinel + Watchman hourly→ miss → SMS desk-loop + invariants→ alarm-watchdog ARMED→ watchdog log overnight reports→ your morning surfaces

Without you: a fleet outage reaches Slack deterministically; a missed agent run reaches your phone. The alarm watchdog is ARMED and acts on its own — its four lanes are pipe 6 below. Everything else lands on the Night Watch / Fixer pages for your morning.

6 · The alarm handles itself alarm-mcp · every 60s · deterministic · ARMED since 2026-08-22
siren?3 min of siren — let it scare→⛔ anyone inside? guard→disarm + re-arm, that partition only→2 strikesa person bypass? (:15)clean cycle restores the sensor→2 strikesa person — door is likely open any trouble?the sorter — 5 ways out→SMS you|Maintenance row|ignore (listed)|unknown → STILL a row tamper?ticket · any hour→SMS Stefan · daytime · ×2→then YOU→clears → row resolves itself

Without you: a tripped siren clears and re-arms itself (max twice, and never with a person inside — the ⛔ guard checks arm state, in_alarm, and open/motion sensors before any command); a stuck bypass is restored; every panel trouble lands somewhere a human can see it — the sorter’s default is now file a ticket, not silence; a tamper runs its own ticket→Stefan→you loop and closes its own row. All deterministic: it keeps working in a full token outage. On the 🚥 Run Monitor since 2026-08-26 — it stamps its own Self-heartbeat every 5 min (row alarm-watchdog (Cloud Run), stale after 15, window 00–24), outside the arm switch, so a stale row means the service is dead rather than merely disarmed. This entry previously read “not on the Sentinel roster, deliberately — not an LLM agent with runs to miss”. That was wrong: determinism is not liveness, the board already carries deterministic jobs, and the only liveness proof was a log line nobody but an agent reads.

Change log

What actually moved, newest first — click an entry to expand. Each takes the colour of the pipe it touched. Add an entry whenever a route, cadence, or failure mode changes — a map nobody updates becomes a map nobody trusts.

  • 2026-08-31

    Process Studio Tickets gains a second engine — Codex (GPT-5.6 Sol) can now run the skill off the Anthropic meter NEW LANE

    Why: Claude subscription tokens were running short and the ticket pass still had to happen. The process-studio-tickets skill already existed in Codex at ~/.codex/skills/process-studio-tickets/SKILL.md — same thin pointer that fetches the live Notion runtime page each run — but Codex had no Notion and no Gmail connector, so it died on step 1.

    What changed: two MCP servers added to ~/.codex/config.toml. notion → hosted https://mcp.notion.com/mcp, OAuth completed. gmail-business → the same local stdio server Claude uses (npx -y @gongrzhe/server-gmail-autoauth-mcp with GMAIL_CREDENTIALS_PATH=/Users/jboon/.gmail-mcp/business-credentials.json), so no re-auth. Backup of the prior config at ~/.codex/config.toml.bak-1787855836.

    The failure mode that cost the time: every Gmail tool call in codex exec came back user cancelled MCP tool call. Not an auth failure and not a timeout — the server answered fine when driven directly over JSON-RPC. Codex was raising an approval elicitation (mcp_tool_call_approval_…) per tool call, and headless exec auto-cancels any prompt it cannot show. Fix: default_tools_approval_mode = "auto" on the server block. Valid values are auto, prompt, writes, approve — strings scraped from the binary suggested allow, which is wrong and fails the whole config load with unknown variant. Set on both gmail-business and notion; Notion reads were passing unprompted (its read tools carry readOnlyHint) but writes — the actual ticket creation — would have hit the same cancel.

    Standing limit — this lane cannot close the loop. The local gmail-business server is message-level only: no search_threads, get_thread, or label_thread. Codex can find the mail and create the Notion ticket; it cannot label the thread processed. That step stays with the hosted business connector (a Claude session) or gets done by hand in Gmail. A Codex-only pass therefore leaves threads unlabelled, which reads as unprocessed to the next sweep — watch for double-processing.

    Run it: codex "process studio tickets". Harmless noise on every run: failed to load models cache: missing field supports_parallel_tool_calls (stale cache, unrelated) and a skills-context-budget warning.

  • 2026-08-31

    Giggster instant bookings have NO reply channel — the welcome message now routes to a staff Action row, same pass NEW ROUTE

    The fact (Junyan): when a guest instant-books on Giggster, the platform generates no guest-chat SMS relay and no reply-to email. There is no door to the renter at all until they write first or give a direct number.

    What changed: the desk used to fall into the wait-for-the-relay pattern (hold a PHONE-* row at Channel = Platform Relay) — correct for ordinary Giggster bookings, wrong here, because the relay is not late, it does not exist. New rule: the first pass that processes an instant booking queues an ✅ Actions to Perform row the same pass — message verbatim in the body, platform-safe wording, asking for the guest’s direct number — and staff send it by hand in the Giggster app chat. Human-confirmed gate; turn logged via log_drop.py after sending. Never a Message Queue row (nothing can dispatch to a platform app; the row just strands).

    Where: dc-canon library/house-rules.md §📵 (commit 38cd8d24) + cross-ref in library/job-2-triage.md (ff03ec26).

  • 2026-08-31 · 21:58Z

    The HTA guard was never actually installed — now it is, and wiring is provable GATE INSTALLED

    The finding: hta_guard.js has lived in dc-canon since it was written, its regression suite passed, and the service README stated “the live guard is called from validate_(row).” None of that was true of the running system. The Apps Script project Message Queue Send Engine had no HtaGuard.gs file at all and validate_ never referenced it, so every How to Access message dispatched unchecked. Discovered only because Junyan pasted the live Code.gs in full. The Himanshi Mehta wrong-studio incident (Aug 15) happened inside that gap.

    What changed: (1) HtaGuard.gs created in the live project from the canon mirror. (2) validate_’s SMS branch now calls validateHta_(row, smsBody_(row.body)) and returns its error — a non-canonical How to Access row flips to Error instead of sending. Non-HTA rows are untouched. (3) Two manual functions added live, neither touching Notion or the sender: testHtaGuard (guard regression) and testHtaWiring (hands validate_ a deliberately broken HTA and logs WIRED / NOT WIRED). Both green: WIRED — validate_ blocked it. (4) The README’s false claim is corrected and dated.

    The lesson worth keeping: a green test suite in the canon repo proves the guard works, never that anything calls it. Mirrored code is not deployed code. Any gate whose enforcement point lives in a UI — Apps Script, Zapier, a Notion automation — needs a wiring probe that exercises the real entry point, not just a unit test of the guard. testHtaWiring is that probe here; run it after every Code.gs edit.

    Watch tomorrow: the booking-day phrase stays optional in the guard during rollout, but any in-flight How to Access row deviating from the canonical template will now Error rather than send. Expected behaviour, first day it can bite.

  • 2026-08-30 · 19:40Z

    The Registrar remembers a late HTA by booking key, not by a window that already closed FAILURE MODE FIX

    The incident: Francisco Delgado’s Studio 527 booking began at 14:00. The first catch-up HTA was queued at 14:30, but hta_queued() searched only rows created no later than the booking start. The 15:00 and 15:30 passes therefore could not see the Registrar’s own 14:30 row and created the same HTA-sweep-0830-1400-527 again. One FBS booking produced three queue rows and three operational texts; the booking and Artist records themselves were not duplicated.

    What changed: dedup now checks the exact Registrar booking key (Artist + full HTA-sweep-MMDD-HHMM-STUDIO code) before applying the older cross-lane historical window. A late catch-up row therefore remains visible after the booking start; the exact-key query keeps a 48-hour lower bound (the title omits the year, so last year’s row must not suppress next year’s booking) and has no upper bound. Pending Review, Ready to Send, Sent, and exact Will Not Send block another draft; Error is excluded server-side and remains repairable. The wider historical-shape check keeps its 48h→start boundary so another booking’s generic “How to Access” row cannot suppress this one. Regression: a row created after start blocks the next pass; prior-year rows do not; Error falls through; 716 event-gate tests pass. No scheduler cadence, auto-send authority, or trigger state changed.

  • 2026-08-30 · 19:26Z

    FBS Monitor shows the AVA/EOB queue beside each booking ROUTE VIEW + FAILURE MODE

    What changed: FBS and Monitor booking cards now carry the approved right-side AVA/EOB pills: the Toronto send time when a matching row is scheduled, neutral QUEUED when the row exists without a time, and red MISSING only when a successful Message Queue read proves an expected row is absent. Matching uses the Artist relation, studio, message kind, and the canonical booking time (AVA = start−2h; EOB = end−15m); an untimed row is shown only when it cannot be confused with a second same-artist booking in that studio.

    Failure and privacy boundary: an unreadable Message Queue, an absent Artist relation, or an ambiguous untimed match renders no dispatch pill rather than manufacturing a missing alarm. Duplicate replacements resolve newest-first. The public page receives only message kind, the three-state display value, and a local clock time — never recipients, bodies, access instructions, relation ids, queue ids, reasoning, or receipts. This is an observability route only: no send status, schedule, trigger, or delivery path changed.

  • 2026-08-29 · 03:29Z

    The bypass watchdog stops counting things it never did FIX + FAILURE MODE

    The live incident: Studio 509A Door became bypassed at 20:16 Toronto. The deterministic alarm-watchdog-bypass job fired and proposed restores at 20:30 and 20:45, but its occupancy guard saw Studio 509B Door open and refused both with acted=False. That was the wrong room boundary: 509A and 509B are separate partitions but share Alarm.com panel prefix 101222561. The guard’s panel-prefix filter treated activity in either room as occupancy in both.

    The second failure made the first permanent: BypassLedger.record() ran while the action was merely being proposed, before the executor’s final safety read. Both refusals therefore consumed the two-attempt cap without one disarm/re-arm command being sent. Every later pass emitted bypass_stuck and stopped retrying. Despite saying “Needs a person at the door,” that action was report-only: it reached Cloud Run logs and no human.

    Fixed and live: shared-panel occupancy now resolves each named sensor to its exact partition; an explicitly-509B door or motion sensor cannot veto a 509A cycle, while a genuinely shared/ambiguous device still blocks both. Attempts move to post-execution accounting and persist only on acted=True, ok=True. The durable ledger is versioned to v2 and discards the old counters once because no v1 counter can prove a real cycle occurred. A bypass that survives two completed cycles now texts on-call once per episode; delivery is durable across cold starts, resets when the sensor clears, and a failed text retries next pass. Commits a59cf42 + 796a6ee, image alarm:0829-796a6ee, revision alarm-mcp-00060-qgq; 277 tests.

    The cadence collision now retries instead of lying green: the 23:30 bypass fire started 22 ms before the every-minute siren tick finished and returned the shared _busy guard in 2.9 ms. Scheduler recorded HTTP 200 even though no bypass decision ran. Busy now returns HTTP 503, and this one job has exactly one retry after 10 seconds (retryCount=1, minBackoffDuration=10s). That is long enough for the ordinary tick to leave and bounded enough not to turn a panel action into an open-ended retry loop.

    Production proof, ordinary schedule: the unchanged 23:45 fire emitted [watchdog] armed=True bypass=True :: bypass_restore:Studio 509A, called it Attempt 1 of 2, and completed acted=True ok=True. The durable v2 ledger then contained exactly 101222561-1: 1; the old refusals were not resurrected. At 23:54 Toronto, nine minutes after execution, Alarm.com no longer reported Sensor Bypassed (Studio 509A Door): all five studios were armed away and all 18 sensors needed no attention. The midnight scheduled bypass pass then reported quiet and reset the durable ledger to empty. Recovery and episode closure proven end to end.

  • 2026-08-27 · ~22:05Z

    Same-day confirmation + HTA become ONE merged text ROUTE CHANGE

    The incident that forced it: Raquel Khan (same-day 527, 20:00) reached the evening with a live door code and no access text. The sweep’s 21:31Z pass queued her first-timer confirmation — whose body promises “Your door code and studio access details will follow” — and then asked “does an HTA exist?”. The HTA-shape filter matched any body containing Door Code (Notion contains is case-insensitive), so the seconds-old confirmation answered yes: hta:found, false positive, nothing sent. The emergency HTA + address follow-up went out by hand at 21:38Z with Junyan’s approval.

    What changed (ruled by Junyan 2026-08-27): (1) same-day, the pair is one merged message — the canonical combined card opening “Thank you for booking with us today!”, keeping the “confirmed for [Day, Date]” line (the dedupe recognises past confirmations by it) and carrying the Artist Care intro / rebooking terms only when owed. The HTA due/not-due decision moved above the confirmation and is made once per pass. (2) The HTA shape now demands the code line — Door Code: with the colon — so a promise of a code can never again impersonate one. Commit 5b306052, revision event-gate-00115-fdn, fp e440e21e1552 verified against HEAD, 703 tests.

    Still open from the incident: the duplicate artist card (Raquel K. code 7368 vs Raquel Khan code 9710, both live on all four panels) awaits Junyan’s merge decision, and her held BC-sweep-0827-2000-527 row is now covered by the sent access text.

  • 2026-08-27 · 20:23Z

    The gate’s last two plaintext credentials move into Secret Manager FIX

    What changed: NOTION_TOKEN and GATE_PATH_TOKEN were the only credentials on event-gate still stored as literal environment values — readable by anyone with console or gcloud describe access, while the other seven already came from Secret Manager. Both now resolve from secrets (notion-token:2, gate-path-token:latest), revision event-gate-00114-qwg. This closes the same class of exposure as yesterday’s path-token rotation, one layer down.

    The secret that would have broken it. A notion-token secret already existed (created 2026-08-16, wired to nothing) — and it was 51 bytes: the 50-char token plus a trailing newline, which Python’s http.client rejects outright as a header value. Pointing the service at it would have killed every Notion read on the gate. Caught by hashing before deploying rather than after: sha256(secret) did not match sha256(live env value), but sha256(secret trimmed) did. A clean 50-byte v2 was minted and the service pins :2, not :latest, so v1 cannot return. Same disease as the 2026-08-16 mcp-auth-token incident (267 failed panel reads); the lesson generalises to byte-check every secret against the known-working value before a service depends on it.

    Deploy trap, new and worth remembering: gcloud refuses to convert an env var from a literal to a secret reference in place — Cannot update environment variable [NOTION_TOKEN] to the given type because it has already been set with a different type. The fix is --remove-env-vars and --update-secrets in the same command (removals apply before updates inside one revision). Splitting it into two commands would be a self-inflicted outage: the gate fails closed, so a revision without GATE_PATH_TOKEN 404s every route including the every-minute emergency poller — precisely the 2026-08-15 shape. The failed first attempt changed nothing (Cloud Run rejects the whole revision), so the retry was free.

    Verified, not assumed: both keys read back as secretKeyRef with no plaintext credential left on the service; /health 200; the emergency poller returned 200 on every tick from 20:23:01 onward (four seconds after the new revision started) and /invariants 200 — a broken token would have 404’d, and a broken Notion token would have surfaced there. Zero errors in the logs since the cutover.

  • 2026-08-27 · ~20:10Z

    The deterministic Sentinel cannot get a runtime sense — so the LLM lane is not retiring DEAD END

    The plan was: mint a TRIGGER_API_TOKEN so sentinel-sweep could read the scheduler’s own trigger registry itself — the last capability keeping the LLM Sentinel lane necessary, and therefore the last thing standing between us and retiring it.

    It cannot be done today. GET /v1/code/triggers is real (it 401s cleanly on a bad token under both Authorization: Bearer and x-api-key) but undocumented, and the project’s anthropic-api-key workspace key is refused by it — with and without the anthropic-beta: experimental-cc-routine-2026-04-01 header the sibling /fire endpoint requires. The docs describe no long-lived non-interactive credential that can LIST triggers: the only documented surface is per-routine /fire with an sk-ant-oat01- token, and claude setup-token’s output is explicitly scoped to model requests and cannot reach connectors.

    The tempting wrong answer: the local Claude Code credential is a user OAuth token in the macOS Keychain, and it would probably work. It is not going into Cloud Run — it is user-scoped and it expires, so the runtime sense would go blind at an unannounced future date without saying so. That is the exact failure mode this watchdog exists to catch; building it that way would be self-defeating.

    Consequence: the runtime sense stays exclusive to the LLM Sentinel lane, whose Claude_Code_Remote connector (scoped list_triggers) is the only working door to the scheduler’s record. That is now the load-bearing reason not to retire that lane — recorded in RUNTIMES.md so a future session does not cheerfully delete it. The larger goal still landed, though: the LLM lane stopped duplicating the heartbeat math when it became an auditor on 2026-08-27, which was the expensive half. What remains is a thin registry-reader plus a grader. Meanwhile the sweep reports the sense as skipped — TRIGGER_API_TOKEN not set rather than guessing.

  • 2026-08-27 · ~19:15Z

    HTA gets a booking-day phrase — the Himanshi “wrong studio” was really a missing date FIX

    Re-investigation overturned the Aug-15 defect ticket: The Host’s “How to Access 527” was the CORRECT day-before HTA for Himanshi Mehta’s real Sun Aug 16 527 booking (she used it; disarmed 527 at 15:40). The defect is that the HTA template carries no date — landing on a day she had a 693 booking, it read as a today-HTA; her “my booking today is at 693 and not 527” was a reasonable reading, and the Responder’s ticket then missed the Sunday booking and filed “no 527 booking at all”. Sweep Feedback row 3bd75032… corrected in place. (Bonus finds: the 11:59 manual resend went to Junyan’s own number, not hers, and said “Sat Aug 16” for a Sunday.)

    What changed: (1) hta_guard.js v2 (dc-canon) accepts + validates an optional booking-day phrase — on Sunday, Aug 16[ at 3:30 PM] — including a weekday↔date consistency check that would have caught the “Sat Aug 16” slip; dateless bodies still pass during rollout so nothing in flight Errors. Tests green. Installed live 2026-08-31 — see the entry above. (2) Skill: Queue FBS Messages HTA Step 4 now MANDATES the phrase (the old “do not add a booking-day phrase” line is gone). (3) New HTR card “We named the wrong studio” (Needs review — an apology never auto-sends), split from the address card per Junyan 2026-08-27; the address card’s Notes cross-link it.

  • 2026-08-27 · ~18:50Z

    Giggster guest chat un-pooled — gmail_pull attributes relay mail by guest name FIX

    What changed: scripts/gmail_pull.py (dc-canon 4a70502a) now files a platform relay’s guest-chat notification under the guest, not the relay: relay_counterparty() reads the name out of “New Message From X” subjects from hello@giggster.com → counterparty giggster-<name>; platform notifications (reviews, payouts) stay pooled under the relay address, which is correct for them. And body_text() strips <style>/<script>/<head> CONTENT before the tag strip and detects HTML anywhere in the part — the old “<html in the first 200 chars” check left 60 lines of Outlook CSS as the stored “body”.

    Backfill done, not just forward: all 239 pooled rows re-fetched from the Gmail API and rewritten in place — 158 re-attributed across 33 guests, 238 CSS bodies replaced with the actual conversation text — then threads re-rendered: threads/giggster-<guest>.md per guest (merges onto the artist page once that contact point is filled in Notion). The hello@giggster.com thread drops 239 → 81 messages, all genuinely the platform’s own. Side discovery, no action needed: the dc-canon local clone is a SPARSE checkout — a “newest gmail row Aug 13” read was the cone, not a dead poller; the poller is healthy.

    Four days of climate shadow — three false alarms, and the spec was the bug FIX

    Reliability: 68 passes, zero failures since the 08-27 id fix; telemetry fresh on both studios every pass; decisions 32 hold / 20 relax / 14 pre-cool / 2 off, and the 901 Liz-window split (36 monitor-only inside, 32 re-assert outside) exactly right. The lane wrote nothing and commanded nothing — shadow held.

    All three alerts it raised were false. Each was empty-room-wont-cool fired on a room sitting AT its setpoint (25.23 vs COOL 25.30; twice 23.24 vs COOL 23.05) — flat, empty, HVAC cooling, which is every literal condition canon step 7a lists. The LLM lane declined all three and wrote down why in the Thermal Log: “Step 7a requires flat/rising ambient WHILE failing to reach setpoint”. Canon never stated that clause — the LLM had been supplying it silently, pass after pass. So this is not a coding slip; it is an under-specified spec, and porting it to code is what made the gap visible. Fixed in both places (1fc5991b): the code now needs the room >1°C above target before it will alert and quotes the setpoint in the alert so a human can check the call, and canon 7a says the condition outright, citing the LLM's own sentence.

    Second bug, found by an alert that printed its own timestamps backwards (18:12→18:05): the sweep was reading the LLM Weatherman's narrative pass rows as thermostat readings. Those rows carry Kind=observation + Source=controller — the same two markers the device logger uses — and their prose (“509 25.23°C mode COOL/HVAC COOLING”) parses as a reading, polluting the trend window. Readings now match on the Entry title SDM reading — . Canon step 2 updated. Self-limiting (it dies with the LLM lane) but it was corrupting the very comparison shadow exists to produce.

    Where this leaves the flip: the live flip has NOT landed — CLIMATE_SWEEP_LIVE is absent from the service, and so are the four SDM secrets, so a flag set alone would have written to Notion while failing every thermostat command. The four days prove reliability but not agreement, because the verdicts were computed on a polluted reading set against a wrong alert rule. Redeploy on 1fc5991b, bind the SDM secrets, then watch a fresh clean window. Suite 739.

  • 2026-08-27 · ~13:30Z

    The Weatherman’s daily report was being created as a page with no database FIXED

    Symptom: the Run Monitor board read the Weatherman as Overdue while every hourly pass ran and filed a perfect report. Same failure on 2026-08-23 and again 2026-08-27; both repaired by hand, and both times the pass log claimed “Run Monitor relation confirmed set in the creation write” — a false claim the agent had no way to detect.

    Cause, two halves, neither written down. (1) The JOB 32 create call never named a parent. Without an explicit data_source_id, Notion returns a standalone private page — no database, no properties — and both the create response and a notion-fetch render it as success; the only tell is an empty <ancestor-path>. (2) The pass-1 self-check was “re-read the report and confirm Run Monitor is set”, which is structurally incapable of catching this: a non-database page has no Run Monitor property, so there is no blank value to find. The check could only ever catch the weaker 2026-08-01→03 failure (relation blank on a real row), not this one.

    Fix (dc-canon commit 4051b95b): library/climate-controller.md step 10 now creates the report with an explicit parent 469a877b-83fa-4387-ac97-94aa656481dd (📊 Workflow Reports) and replaces the fetch-based check with one by-URL SQL row check asserting both that the row exists and that Run Monitor is non-empty, plus the notion-move-pages repair path. library/autonomy.md §1 carries the same rule fleet-wide — any report bot can hit this. Both note that this single verification query is a deliberate exception to the never-SQL rule, which governs the per-pass dedup read only.

    Why it recurred: the 08-23 Sweep Feedback row (3c575032…) already contained this exact diagnosis and this exact suggested fix. It sat at New for four days and was never applied to canon — so the identical failure came back on 08-27 and got hand-patched a second time. Both rows now marked Applied with the commit noted. Mirror verified end-to-end: git → library_mirror_sync → projection page, synced version 96a9026e8d7f.

    “Will Not Send” now means no — the Registrar had been re-drafting cancelled access textsFAILURE MODE · CLOSED

  • 2026-08-27 · 02:30Z

    The leaked gate token is dead — rotated with zero downtime on the emergency laneSECURITY · CLOSED

    Moving the token into a header stopped new leakage but did not close the exposure — the old value still sat in roughly 30 days of Cloud Logging, and a stolen token works just as well in a header as in a URL. It is now rotated and the leaked value returns 404.

    The obstacle was that the token lives in two places that cannot change in the same instant: the service environment and six Cloud Scheduler job headers — one of which is the every-minute emergency SMS poller. Swapping the env alone would 404 every caller until the last job caught up. So the gate learned a second env var, GATE_PATH_TOKEN_PREV (0be6be9a): during a rotation both the outgoing and incoming token open the door, and the jobs move across one at a time.

    Sequence, each step verified before the next: deploy PREV support → one atomic env update setting the new token and PREV=old → confirm both tokens return 200 and garbage returns 404 → rotate the five low-risk jobs, emergency poller last → verify all six job headers by sha256 rather than trusting the write → remove PREV → confirm the old token is refused. Zero auth failures across the entire rotation, and the poller never missed a tick.

    Standing rule: leaving PREV set would silently keep a retired token alive forever, so removing it is the last step of a rotation, not an optional tidy-up — a test pins that.

  • 2026-08-27 · 01:40Z

    FBS board stops calling an unobservable arrival a no-showFAILURE MODE

    The panel can only witness an arrival as a disarm. On a tight transition the outgoing renter walks out without arming, the panel stays disarmed, and the incoming renter has nothing to disarm — so the board painted a red no arrival / MISSING on someone standing in the room (2026-08-26: Fabio Hernandez, 509A 20:15, right behind Daniel’s 19:00–20:00).

    fbs-monitor/build.py gains a fourth matching pass covering both unobservable-arrival shapes: the last panel event before start is a keypad disarm (nobody armed — remote disarms prove an open panel, not presence, so canon holds), or the panel was armed with the door sensor bypassed (the actual 2026-08-26 case: Ishfaaq armed 509A 19:06, bypass notice 19:07, so Daniel and Fabio both entered without touching the keypad — pass 4 consumes the TELUS BYPASS alerts the builder already parsed). The arrival is assumed on-time and rendered on both pages as an amber warning naming the cause — HH:MM in · door bypassed / HH:MM in · forgot to arm — never as a clean measured time, and the MISSING flag no longer fires. A real no-show behind an unarmed or bypassed panel is now invisible by design — the trade Junyan chose; a clean arm with no bypass still produces the honest red flag.

  • 2026-08-27 · 01:35Z

    The smart watchdog stops being a watchdog and becomes the robot’s examinerROLE CHANGED

    Two watchdogs doing the same job meant a real problem would text Junyan twice. Since the deterministic sentinel-sweep went live it has owned the routine job — hourly, 24/7, zero tokens — so sentinel-v3-2026-08-27 retires the LLM lane’s duplicate role and gives it three jobs it can do that the robot cannot:

    • Dead-man. The sweep cannot report its own death. The auditor reads the sweep’s rolling report and treats a gap over 90 minutes as SWEEP SILENT — SMS, and it takes the watchdog job back for that pass. This is the role the retired Watchman used to play for the Sentinel.
    • Audit. It still computes the whole fleet’s health independently — an auditor that trusts what it audits is worthless — then compares against the sweep’s ALARMS: ledger. Agree → log only, no text (the sweep already sent one). It found a miss the sweep did not → SMS, the dangerous direction. The sweep alarmed on something it judges healthy → defect row, no SMS.
    • Runtime watch. Unchanged and still exclusive: the sweep has no trigger-API token, so it is blind to whether each bot’s timer is actually switched on.

    The audit found its first bug within the hour — in the LLM, not the robot. At 20:09 the old Sentinel texted Junyan that qa-review was ~117h overdue, then retracted at 21:08 and texted again. Both were wrong: qa-review's own log shows an unbroken hourly run 🕒 00:00 … 🕒 21:00, including 20:00 — nine minutes before the alarm — and the deterministic sweep said all clear at every pass.

    The cause is subtler than a page size. The prompt read the reports through a view sorted by Started At, 40 rows, has_more: true. A rolling daily report is started once at 04:00 but edited every hour — so as the day fills with newer runs it sinks below the cut while its heartbeat is minutes old. The Sentinel never saw the current report, found a stale one from five days earlier, and did the arithmetic on that. A first fix that merely raised the page to 100 was wrong too: a bigger page only moves the cut. v3.2 sorts by the field actually being measured — Last Edited, via SQL, never the view — fetches rolling reports by title, and requires a targeted per-run query to confirm every candidate before it counts. Plus a smell test: an hourly lane reported 117 hours late is presumptively a bad read, because nobody misses five days of an hourly job unnoticed.

    The agreement record is the point. Every pass logs audit: AGREE or a disagreement, so the decision to eventually switch the LLM lane off rests on evidence rather than nerve. Known gap, written into the prompt: the auditor sleeps 23:00–07:00 Toronto while the sweep runs on, so a sweep death overnight is not caught until the 07:00 pass.

  • 2026-08-27 · 00:05Z

    The gate’s only password was sitting in the logs — it now travels in a headerSECURITY · ROTATION PENDING

    Every event-gate route took its token as part of the web address, and Cloud Run copies the full address of every request into its access log. That token is the only thing standing in front of the SMS poller, the advancement loop, the booking sweep and the emergency lane — so anyone who could read the project’s logs could lift it and call any of them. Found while reading sentinel-sweep logs for an unrelated reason.

    Fixed in two deploys, callers migrated in between. 48e866b7 taught every route to accept the token in an X-Gate-Token header (headers are not logged) while still accepting the old way; the six Cloud Scheduler jobs were then moved one at a time and each verified firing; aadf65b0 deleted the token-in-URL routes outright. A request carrying the real token in the address now returns 404, and a test fails loudly if anyone ever adds such a route back. The emergency poller was migrated last and confirmed green on the next minute tick.

    ⚠️ Not finished: this stops new leakage only. The old token still sits in roughly 30 days of historical logs, so it has to be rotated before the exposure is genuinely closed — a leaked token works just as well in a header as in a URL.

    Drop sweep LIVE — and Buzz membership is two doors, not oneNEW

    Live and scheduled. Cloud Scheduler job drop-sweep (*/15 * * * *, America/Toronto) is ENABLED against POST /drop-sweep with header auth. Proven end to end on a real staff drop: the dry-run parsed caption + one image; the live pass filed the 📸 row with the media re-uploaded into Notion and reacted 📥; a second pass returned filed:0, deduped:1, so the Buzz-Event-ID dedup holds against the real relay, not just the tests. Env set: BUZZ_RELAY_URL=wss://danceannex.communities.buzz.xyz, DROP_SWEEP_CHANNEL=04d5c93f-bcb1-4edf-b868-105dffbe408d, key from Secret Manager.

    The trap, for whoever adds the next Buzz agent: membership is two separate grants. Adding the pubkey to the channel (buzz channels add-member, or the app’s Add people) is not enough — every read still returns 403 relay_membership_required, because the hosted relay keeps its own member list. The second grant is Settings → Invites → Invite to community in Buzz Desktop, and the buzz CLI has no command for it (no members, no community subcommand) — it is a Block-hosting operation, UI-only, owner-only. Two more sharp edges met on the way: the app’s member search only finds identities the relay already knows, so a brand-new agent key looks like “No matching people”; and gcloud scheduler jobs create takes --headers, not --update-headers (that is the update-only spelling).

    ⚠ Still pending, deliberately: the Concierge repoint. JOB 4 still reads Slack, so 📸 rows are accumulating unread — capture is live, processing is not. Slack remains the live drop channel; do not onboard staff to Buzz drops yet. Also open: no 🛑 Run Monitor row for the sweep (15-min cadence, stale after 45), and GATE_PATH_TOKEN was echoed to a terminal by a gcloud arg-parse error — it should be rotated with the pre-existing log-leak rotation.

  • 2026-08-26 · 23:30Z

    The Send-Timing Verifier was querying with a timestamp format Notion never returnsFAILURE MODE

    A false all-clear hiding in a separator character. The v4 prompt told the verifier to bound its window with WHERE "Created" >= '<12h ago UTC>' written as ISO with a T. Notion returns and compares Created as 2026-08-24 02:07:26Z — a space separator. A T bound matches nothing and returns {"results":[]}, which is indistinguishable from “the Host never ran.” Caught on the 2026-08-26 22:19Z live run, which only reached a true answer by falling back to a 72h pull by hand.

    Fixed in the stored prompt (RemoteTrigger trig_013TgaXwW2twipdmo2NUrHwa, re-pushed 23:29Z; cron 0 9 * * *, enabled state and all three connectors verified intact). Two additions: a TIMESTAMP FORMAT TRAP note giving the exact format, and a ZERO-ROW RULE — an empty or implausibly small result is a suspect query, never a finding, and must be re-run against a wide 72h window before anything is concluded from it. Also folded in the notion-create-pages gotcha the same run hit: every field, Booking ref included, must sit inside properties.

    That was the second false-all-clear path in one run — the first, late-fire handling, was closed 2026-08-25. Both share a shape: a window that could not have contained the Host’s batch, reported as clean.

  • 2026-08-26 · 20:20Z

    Every brand-new renter was dying at the verify probe — fixedBUG

    The Daniel Thevarasalingam case: paid first-ever booking at 13:09, 509A 19:00 tonight. The Registrar caught it in one pass, built the Artist card and contact points, then filed /provision HTTP 409 and paged Junyan — and kept 409ing on all five passes since, each in ~1.4 s. That latency was the tell: a real provision waits up to 60 s for panel sync, so no create was ever attempted.

    Root cause: alarm-mcp’s /provision stamps HTTP 409 on every non-pass verdict — including the verify_only probe’s honest “this person is not provisioned yet” (manual_no_match) — and the sweep’s _alarm_call raised on any non-2xx. So the probe itself threw, the chain died before the create-and-retry logic (yesterday’s 409-retry guards the CREATE, which never ran), and every brand-new renter failed while returning renters passed the probe and kept the lane looking alive. Fix: the probe passes read_status=(409,) and the 409 body is parsed as the answer it is. Daniel himself was provisioned by hand (user 248425645, code 6259, verified on all 4 panels, allow_permanent) with 3 h to spare. Deployed rev event-gate-00091-6ss, fingerprint bd6089f480e8 verified; 586 tests pass.

    Desk Invariants stops manufacturing its own backlog — B sees both worklists, dedup honors CancelledFAILURE MODE FIXED · DEPLOY PENDING

    The single largest Actions-board inflow pipe was the checker itself: 91 Raised by = Desk Invariants rows since Aug 12, root-caused to two canon/code parity gaps.     Fix (dc-canon c1c24975, 584 tests pass): next_action_covered_ids() treats an open Proposed/Ready/Mismatch queue row as covering its ticket for B, and a failed queue read suppresses B for the pass rather than false-filing; board_escalations() dedupes per (invariant code, ticket id) against open AND Cancelled self rows — open → bump the run count in place, Cancelled → never re-raise, and a cancelled E cannot gag a later B on the same ticket. Not yet live: the Cloud Run deploy needs Junyan (services/event-gate/deploy.sh) — until then the old checker keeps filing. Part of the 2026-08-26 “kill classes, not rows” strategy (inflow ~34 rows/day, ~45% machine chatter).

    The mystery file-dropper — Dropbox Sign’s Drive forwarder deactivated, NOT yet confirmed fixedROUTE REMOVED · UNCONFIRMED

    ⚠ Status, stated honestly: the cause is STRONGLY suspected, not proven. The 60-minute recurrence below was called “confirmed hourly polling” on a single clean interval — then the next copy arrived 15 minutes after a delete, not 60, and after the integration was switched off. So the interval theory is wrong and the attribution is unverified. The deactivation may still be correct; the 17:01Z copy most likely predates it by minutes. The real test is the next agreement signed: if no raw file appears in the root for it, the fix held; if one does, it was never Dropbox Sign and the connected-apps list at myaccount.google.com/connections is where to look next. Deleting the copy is deliberately STOPPED — each delete appeared to provoke the next re-create, which destroys the signal. Update this entry when a signing settles it.

    Raw Dance_Annex_Long_Term_Rental_Agreement-<signer-email>.pdf files kept appearing in the ROOT of the filing folder, un-triaged and un-named, alongside the copies DocIntake files properly. Junyan was confident nothing was configured to do it, and every automation surface agreed: IFTTT (10 applets, all thermostat), Zapier (zero workflows), every sibling .gs in the Send Engine project (only Code.gs:566 touches Drive, and it reads), and Google Drive for Desktop (not installed on this Mac). On that evidence the working theory became “a person clicking Save to Drive” — which was wrong, and the folder said so within the hour.

    What broke the case was deleting one and watching. The file returned twenty minutes later under a NEW file id, then again at exactly 60 minutes after that (15:46:07Z → 16:46:12Z, third distinct id). A person does not do that. An hourly job that re-creates whatever is missing from its target folder does — and that also explains the shape of the pile: only recent signings were present, because anything outside its window was never re-pushed, which is why the Aug 21 file stayed gone while the Aug 24 one kept coming back.

    Culprit: Dropbox Sign’s own Google Drive integration — Dropbox Sign → Settings → Integrations → Google Drive, “Forward signed emails and requested signature documents to Google Drive.” Not an automation platform at all, which is why sweeping the automation platforms cleared everything and found nothing. Deactivated by Junyan 2026-08-26, confirmed by the button flipping to Activate.

    The diagnostic that pointed the right way was Drive metadata, not the automation lists: file owner = thedanceannex@gmail.com with that account as the ONLY permission. Nobody was pushing files in from outside — something was writing AS the studio account, which narrows the suspect list from “anything on the internet” to “apps this account has authorised”.

    Safe to remove: DocIntake reads the Dropbox Sign Gmail notification, never the Drive copy, so the intake lane is untouched — the mail still arrives exactly as before. The forwarded copy had been pure duplication since the lane went live: every agreement stored twice, once filed under the house convention and once raw in the root. Standing lesson: “we have no automation that does this” was true as stated — the culprit was a setting inside a SaaS product, a surface nobody thinks of as automation. When every automation platform comes back clean, widen to product-level integrations before concluding a human did it.

    Document intake goes live and files 15 — then shows its naming was guessingLANE LIVE + SCHEMA + DEFECT

    The lane had never filed anything. Three causes stacked, each hiding the next. DOC_INTAKE_ENABLED was unset, so every run returned on the first line — an execution log with zero lines, which reads as “ran fine”. There was also no trigger on docIntakeTick; had one existed it would have fired hourly at 0 % error rate doing nothing, which is worse than no trigger, because the trigger screen would have looked healthy. Once enabled, the real fault surfaced: the 📎 Docs data source was not shared with the Send Engine Notion integration — 404 object_not_found on every row write. The 🎨 Artist DB was already shared, proven by docFindArtist_ running two lines earlier without throwing. Connection added; the 60-day sweep then filed 15 documents in 3m16s, including the three Duuo certificates stuck since 15–18 July.

    Then the filenames gave the lane away. Verified against Drive, not the log — the log said 15, the folder said what kind. Dropbox Sign titles use the ACRONYM (“Dance Annex AAP Agreement”), never the words “Annual Artist Plan”, which is all docAgreementToken_ matched. Kyle FitzGerald and Dennis Jun both fell through to the generic agreement fallback. Skedda tags Kyle (AAP) on every booking and Dennis was already hand-filed _AAP — so the evidence was there to be read and had not been. Acronym patterns added, case-sensitive so a lowercase “aap” inside a word cannot false-positive.

    Three byte-identical duplicates were created, by matching identity on FILENAME. A corrected token or a re-parsed term changes the name, and getFilesByName then sees a new document: Dennis Jun (437,083 B), Alison Murray (811,022 B), Kateryna Zozulia (590,511 B) each got a second copy. Identity is now byte size + holder. Dennis’s duplicate would not have existed had the acronym fix landed first — the name would have matched and the file been reused. Junyan deleted the pairs; two more (Shamir Panchal ×2) are owned by the personal account and the connector cannot touch them. A fourth was older: Geunseop Lee_rental-agreement, the invented token from the first cut, still sitting there since 10 Aug.

    Naming ruling (Junyan, 2026-08-26): amendments are _<plan> (amendment N). “Amended” is a modifier, not a plan type — filing as _Amended threw away which plan the artist is on, the one thing the token exists to record. N is read from what is already filed, per holder per plan, so it keeps advancing even though an amendment usually changes the term (Alison’s moved Jul 1–Sep 30 → Aug 1–Nov 30 in the same document, which is why the script’s dates and the hand-filed name disagreed — both read the same PDF, one row apart). Renamed: Kyle → _AAP, Alison → (amendment 1), Kateryna → (amendment 1) and (amendment 2).

    ⚠ Four of Kateryna’s 2026 documents are signed but were never filed — Amended 13 Jan, Amended 12 Feb, Long Term Rental 13 Apr, Amended 16 Apr. So her amendment numbering reflects what is on file, not her history; if those are filed later the numbers are wrong. The gap is unlikely to be hers alone: anything signed before late June is unfiled unless done by hand. A full-year sweep exceeded the 6-minute cap (60 days alone takes 3m16s), so DOC_LOOKBACK_UNTIL now adds an older bound and a backfill runs in slices — 120d/60d, 180d/120d, 250d/180d. Not run; left where it stands on Junyan’s call.

    ⚠ State to carry forward: the Apps Script copy is BEHIND send-engine/DocIntake.gs. The acronym fix, the amendment convention, content dedup and DOC_LOOKBACK_UNTIL are all local only. Re-paste before the next run or it repeats today’s duplicates. No trigger is installed, deliberately — an hourly job minting near-duplicates is how you find a naming bug sixty files late. Also unresolved: something other than this lane still drops raw Dance_Annex_Long_Term_Rental_Agreement-<email>.pdf files into the folder ROOT (one arrived 24 Aug), so every agreement is stored twice until that automation is found. And the Sales - Agreements label search still returns {} while from:hellosign.com returns instantly — second confirmation that Gmail’s label index is broken, not the labelling; sweeps must key on sender + subject.

    Root cause of the Aug 9 board gap: the Planner builds from the Artist DB, not SkeddaDIAG

    The open question from the notification-blackout incident is closed. Two 2026-08-09 bookings — Isabella Alves and Wanetta Chu — never got FBS board rows from the nightly T−3 build, while ten siblings on the same day built fine. No filter bug, no dropped page: The Planner cannot see Skedda at all. Its own prompt reads “this pass reaches Notion + Google Calendar only — no Alarm.com, no Skedda”, and Step T2 tells it to escalate not-in-Artist-DB holders to the For Staff checklist. A renter with no Artist row therefore yields no board row — only a line in a run report. The board build is transitively dependent on the mail-driven ticket pipeline that creates those Artist rows, so breaking the mail silently under-builds the board, and the board row is upstream of the HTA.

    The control that kills the obvious wrong answer. Himanshi Mehta booked on Aug 1 — during the blackout — and still got a nightly row, because her Artist record dated from June 19. Every Aug 9 booking with a pre-existing Artist row built; both without one did not. The discriminator is not when the booking was made, it is whether the renter existed in Notion. Wanetta’s and Ali Ahmad’s Artist rows were stamped 2026-08-07 18:21:16Z — five minutes after the blackout lifted at 18:16Z — and ten catch-up board rows followed at 19:45:54Z. The repair happened on Aug 7; the Aug 25–26 pass re-verified it rather than discovering it.

    Partly fixed already, and the gap that remains. The Registrar (booking-sweep, live 2026-08-20) reads Skedda directly and re-derives Artist row, code, board row and confirmation every pass — the same blackout today would self-repair inside 30 minutes. But only within SWEEP_HORIZON_DAYS = 14. Past 14 days the Planner still owns the build, and still depends on the Artist DB. Isabella’s Aug 14, Salia’s Aug 16, Tamina’s Aug 17 and Saskia’s Aug 18 rows all sat in that uncovered band.

    ⚠️ Do not patch this by having the Planner create Artist rows — with no Skedda connector it cannot see the holder record it would need. The honest options are a Skedda read for the Planner, a wider Registrar horizon (declined on policy 2026-08-25), or routing the not-in-Artist-DB escalation somewhere that alarms instead of a run-report checklist. That escalation working exactly as designed is how this stayed invisible for 11 days. No production change made — diagnosis only; canon library/gotchas.md 7c3154ca.

  • 2026-08-26 · 14:52Z

    Siren runs 3 minutes, not 90 seconds — and the Scheduler deadline had to move with itALARM

    Junyan’s call: “let it scream for 3 minutes not 90 seconds.” The 90s default was set 2026-08-12, before a single real trip had been watched end to end. siren_seconds 90 → 180, image alarm:siren-180-2026-08-26.

    The part that was not obvious, and would have bitten: the executor holds its Cloud Run request open for the whole siren wait, and both watchdog Scheduler jobs were created with an attemptDeadline of 180s. At a 180-second siren the tick would run ~200–220s and Scheduler would abort it part-way — and the place it aborts is between the disarm and the re-arm, leaving a studio open with the log reading fine. That is the single worst outcome this system can produce. Both jobs raised to 360s and the Cloud Run request timeout 300s → 420s, in the same change and before the deploy.

    A test now pins the coupling (test_the_siren_wait_fits_inside_the_scheduler_deadline) so the next raise cannot land without someone rereading that paragraph. 258 tests pass; ticks verified quiet on the new revision.

  • 2026-08-26 · 14:20Z

    The alarm watchdog joins the 🚥 Run Monitor — reversing Monday’s callMAP

    Junyan asked the question that broke the old answer: “how can I know if it is off or malfunctioning? The run monitor is where I go, and there are other deterministic code on there.” On 2026-08-24 this map recorded the opposite ruling — that the watchdog stays off the roster because it is not an LLM agent with runs to miss, and the every-60s [watchdog] log line is its heartbeat. That was wrong. The log line is real evidence, but only to someone holding a gcloud logging read command, which made the true answer to “is it off?” “ask Claude”. Determinism is not liveness — watchlist-refresh and The Mechanic are both deterministic, both went dark, and both are watched.

    Now: alarm_monitor/heartbeat.py stamps Self-heartbeat on roster row alarm-watchdog (Cloud Run) — cadence 5-min, stale after 15, window 00–24. Throttled to one write per 5 min (the tick is 60s; stamping each would be 1,440 Notion writes/day), non-fatal, and the throttle clock advances only on a successful write so a Notion blip retries next tick instead of ageing the board itself. Stamped outside the WATCHDOG_ARMED gate, like the escalation and tamper lanes: pausing the arm switch stops the watchdog acting on panels, not running — so a stale row means genuinely dead, never merely disarmed. Deployed heartbeat-2026-08-26, first live stamp verified 14:18Z; 256 tests pass.

    ⚠️ Green means the tick is alive, not that the alarm system is healthy. It cannot see a tampered panel, a faulted sensor, or a siren suppressed as needing a human — those keep their own lanes (SMS for critical, tickets for the rest).

  • 2026-08-26 · 03:45Z

    Send-Timing Verifier was emailing the personal inbox — and a rate-limited fire silently ran 16h lateROUTINE FIX

    Two defects in one routine, ⏱️ Send-Timing Verifier trig_013TgaXwW2twipdmo2NUrHwa (cloud, cron 0 9 UTC = 05:00 Toronto). Both found from the Aug 24 report Junyan received.

    1. Wrong mailbox. Step 6 of the prompt hardcoded junyan.boon@gmail.com. It sends through the business Gmail connector, so every daily report arrived from info@danceannex.ca to the personal inbox — an operational fleet alert filed with personal mail, against the CLAUDE.md rule that agent alerts live in the business mailbox. Now emails thedanceannex@gmail.com, with an explicit do-not-use note on the personal address so a future edit does not quietly restore it. The Needs-Care card pipeline keys off the business-side message id, so it is unaffected.

    2. A late fire can produce a false all-clear. The Aug 24 fire landed on time at 09:09Z and died in one second: rate_limit: rejected (seven_day) — org monthly spend limit. A Try again at 01:27Z Aug 25 (21:27 Toronto) ran it ~16 hours late. The prompt’s fixed “last 12 hours” window would then have started at 13:28Z — after the Host’s 14:05Z batch — so it would have audited zero rows and reported clean. That run caught it and widened the window on its own initiative; the next one might not. Late-fire handling is now written into step 1: read the real Toronto clock first, and if materially past 05:00, widen to 00:00Z of the Toronto day plus 72h context and state the run time and window in the email. Never report a clean run off a window that could not have contained the Host’s batch.

    The same long-tail pattern appears on Aug 17, 22 and 23 — assume those were rate-limited-and-retried too. Watch for: the rate limit itself is not fixed here, only made survivable. A fire that is rejected and never manually retried leaves that day genuinely unaudited, with no alert at all.

  • 2026-08-26 · 15:30Z

    The watchdog goes deterministic (shadow) — and the Watchman retiresMONITOR

    Why. The Sentinel is an LLM run that burns tokens hourly — and the August outage began with a trigger switched off to conserve tokens. A watchdog that costs tokens dies of exactly the disease it monitors for. So its staleness math is now ported to plain code: sentinel_sweep.py in event-gate, fired hourly at :07 by Cloud Scheduler, zero tokens, runs through any token outage.

    Shadow, deliberately. It reads everything and writes nothing — no SMS, no Sweep Feedback, no board stamps; run(live=True) raises. Verdicts land only in Cloud Run logs, to be compared against the LLM Sentinel for a few days. The flip to live is a separate, human-approved change; the LLM trigger stays enabled until then. Kill switch SENTINEL_SWEEP_DISABLED=1. The §2b runtime sense is coded but gated on a TRIGGER_API_TOKEN secret that does not exist yet — it reports itself skipped rather than guessing.

    Its first two fires each caught a real bug — which is what shadow mode is for: (1) bare-date Completed At values (IPR/Overtime style) parse timezone-naive and crashed the heartbeat max(); (2) rows that heartbeat via Self-heartbeat instead of reports (the new alarm-watchdog row) read as 685 minutes missing while their stamp was 3 minutes old. Both fixed with regression tests (suite 573); third fire: 16/16 healthy, matching the LLM lane.

    The Watchman is retired (Junyan, 2026-08-26): trigger disabled, board row Paused. Its question — is the Sentinel alive? — now has a daily owner instead of an hourly one: Morning Text v2.2’s WATCHDOG-SILENT check leads the SMS with 🔴 if the Sentinel shows no signal since 07:00. Named trade, worth remembering: the Watchman was the only watcher outside the GCP failure domain, and once the deterministic sweep goes live, the sweep, its scheduler and most of what it watches all share that domain. v2.1 the same day fixed the Morning Text’s first false alarm — it called a healthy Weatherman “stuck” off a frozen agent-written Status; it now checks Last Edited and the 🕒 body log first, the same guard the Sentinel has carried since Aug 6.

  • 2026-08-26 · 01:47Z

    The Run Monitor gains a second sense: it now watches RUNTIMES, not just personasMONITOR

    The hole. Every watcher on this fleet asked one question — “was a report filed?” — and that question is forgeable. From 2026-08-22 to 08-25 the Concierge’s cloud trigger was disabled (~21 missed fires) while a Codex fallback kept filing The Concierge — Rolling Daily reports under the same persona name. The Sentinel and The Watchman both ran, both stayed green, and both were correct. “The Concierge” was a persona label, not a runtime, so nothing on the board could tell a live lane from a dead one wearing its name.

    The fix. 🚥 Run Monitor gains three columns — Runtime (the trig_… id, or gha: for GitHub Actions lanes), Runtime state, Runtime checked — and every Live row is now stamped with the thing that actually fires it. The Sentinel (sentinel-v2-2026-08-25) gains §2b: one read-only list_triggers call per pass against the scheduler’s own record, which no agent can fake by writing a report. Disabled, deleted, or a next_run_at stuck in the past → SMS + Sweep Feedback, deduped separately from heartbeat misses and explicitly exempt from the §3a body cross-check — a heartbeat gap is an inference that can be wrong, a disabled trigger is ground truth. It fires even when the heartbeat is green; that pairing is the loudest thing it can say, because it means something other than the intended runtime is filing that lane’s reports.

    Two traps found by probing rather than assuming (🧪 Runner Capability Probe, repurposed — still disabled, manual-run only). Both are written into §2b so they are never rediscovered: (a) the cloud list_triggers route omits enabled when it is false rather than sending false — 21 routines, 19 true, and the 2 with the key absent were exactly the 2 disabled ones. Any watcher reading absent as “unknown, skip” is blind to precisely what it exists to catch. (b) the listing paginates and truncates silently — the desktop API route returned 20 with has_more=true and accepts no cursor, while the cloud route with limit:100 returns all 21 in one page. A dead trigger past the cut would have been invisible forever.

    Privilege, deliberately narrow. The Sentinel had no runtime visibility at all (Notion + Phone.com only), so it gained the Claude_Code_Remote connector — scoped permitted_tools: ["list_triggers"] and verified on read-back. An unattended watchdog that could disable production triggers is a worse problem than the one being solved; it can read the roster and shout, nothing more. If a runtime is off on purpose, the fix is a human setting that row to Paused.

    Still open. The two GitHub Actions lanes (qa-review, watchlist-refresh) are ticked by cron-job.org and this API cannot see them — marked N/A, still heartbeat-only. The Analyst is resolved: its runtime was never missing, only invisible — it was the one routine past the pagination cut. The Sentinel’s first §2b pass named it on sight (trig_01JDu3k596RWLPRvdnBqoG45, enabled, fires ~04:06 Toronto), which is the whole point of asking the scheduler instead of inferring. Worth recording that the row had been marked “unknown, not proven absent” rather than “has no runtime” — the stronger claim would have been wrong. Verified live 02:08Z: 13/13 checkable triggers stamped Live, no Sweep Feedback row, no SMS, and the pass logged all clear (runtimes 13/13) — the count is deliberate, since silence cannot prove §2b ran.

    event-gate DEPLOYED (108 + the 409 retry), and the Morning Text moves to 08:45 to carry the handoff verdictDEPLOY + CADENCE CHANGE

    Deployed on Junyan's instruction: revision event-gate-00075-8jk, then -00076-s4x. One release carries ticket 108 (JOB 2's self-serve-noise rule narrowed to known-artist-only — built 08-25 03:05Z, undeployed until now) and the 409 re-pick. Shipping them together is safe because each reverts on its own switch without a redeploy: SWEEP_SAMEDAY_DISABLED=1 for 108, SWEEP_PROVISION_ATTEMPTS=1 for the retry. Neither is set; both are live. If the release misbehaves, flip ONE at a time — same service, so reasoning about which half caused it is otherwise guesswork.

    Proof the right code is running, and the trap in it. /health returns code_fingerprint (computed from the source at import) and git_sha. The fingerprint served — 474134dedb13 — matches python version.py on the local tree, which is what actually proves the deploy. git_sha proved nothing, and briefly lied: it is only the GIT_SHA env var, and a --source deploy that does not pass it inherits the previous revision's value. Post-deploy it read a25606b8 — a real but older commit — sitting confidently next to correct code. Corrected by hand to 4da13b48. Always pass --update-env-vars GIT_SHA=$(git rev-parse --short HEAD). RUNTIMES.md's line saying event-gate has no version endpoint was already stale and is now replaced with this.

    The Morning Text moves 07:00 → 08:45 and becomes the push half of the Night Watch. Junyan's call, after asking the right question: why are there two morning surfaces at all? The console had the correct verdict for three mornings running and no way to reach a phone; the text reaches the phone daily and had no verdict. They were split for a real reason — at 07:00 the text fired before the Concierge's 08:10 pass, so the NW-HANDOFF-V1 marker did not exist yet. Moving the text past the console dissolves that. Prompt v2 adds a non-skippable step (d): read the marker itself, not the console page (the marker is the authority, the page is a rendering, and 5 minutes is too thin a margin to depend on a publish), and rank the verdict second only to security. The heartbeat now records the raw marker line, which is what lets tomorrow's fire say “3rd morning running” instead of just “red”.

    What this deliberately does NOT do. It does not add a second SMS lane — that was the earlier proposal and it was worse: one push channel is the point. It also does not fix the deeper blindness, that 🚩 Run Monitor tracks a persona rather than a runtime, so a dead cloud lane is invisible while a fallback files under the same name. That stays open, and is now the only thing on the watch list that a green board can still hide.

    Cost, recorded honestly: day-ahead information now arrives 1h45m later. Harmless on a day like today (first booking 19:15); it bites on a morning with an 08:00 booking and an access problem. The alternative was moving the Concierge's first pass to ~06:00 so the handoff finishes before Junyan wakes — still the better long-term shape, and not done today.

  • 2026-08-25 · 23:40Z

    The ADT email becomes a second witness — the board now checks the websocket against it, and says so when they disagreeGUARD

    The feed is alive. Ruled permanently dead on 20 Aug (entry below), reactivated as a backup by Junyan on 24 Aug, and measured today: ~200 named messages in seven days, still landing on Artist Care - ADT. On the same event it agrees with the websocket to the second — Studio 509A was Disarmed by Ivanka Moskaliuk, 24 Aug 23:05:22Z, both sources. Two independent witnesses to the one fact the board cannot re-derive from anything else: who.

    What was built: reconcile_mail_against_doors() in fbs-monitor build.py, run on every ~15-minute rebuild that has both feeds. For each named mail event it asks one question — did the websocket see it too? matched is the expected case. in_gap (a recorded gap covers that moment) is not a defect but the only routine proof the coverage machinery is honest. missed — the socket claimed to be listening and has no record — is a hole inside a window reported as clean, which is precisely the failure the coverage guard exists to prevent. That one alone goes loud, into the rebuild note, naming the studio, time and person and telling the reader to treat those windows as uncovered for billing. Result also rides in armFeed.mailCheck for the Alarms tab.

    Deliberately one-directional, and deliberately quiet. Door-only events are never reported: the socket legitimately sees more than the mail (remote arms, doors, motion), and mail silence is as likely to be a throttle as a miss — canon’s standing ban on treating an absent ADT email as evidence is what makes the reverse direction unusable. Mail older than covers_since is skipped, because the ledger prunes at 72 h and cannot speak for time it never held. Tolerance is 5 minutes against an observed 0 s: a false hole costs an investigation, a missed one costs a single pass.

    Why it can never be more than a check. The mail carries no door open/close — verified, not assumed — so it cannot recover what a socket blink actually loses, which is the departure. And it throttles itself without warning: four “High activity … blocked or limited for up to 24 hours” notices on 24–25 Aug alone, triggered by exactly the busy evenings when you would most want a second source. A backstop that disappears under load is not a backstop.

    Paper trail: fbs-monitor 1b9dd306 (build.py + nine cases in test_titles.py, 29 tests green); canon house-rules.md already carries the 24 Aug reactivation and its limits — this implements them rather than changing them.

    Same-day auto-send sent 9 unwanted code texts on its first afternoon — HELD at 20:47Z, three gates shipped at 21:05ZINCIDENT + FIX

    The incident. The fully-robotic same-day window (12289d34, live 16:49Z) texted door codes to nine renters by 20:31Z. No wrong codes, no security exposure — every code was panel-verified the same pass and went by direct SMS to the renter’s own number. But three regulars (Chelly, Tamina, Sharon) received the same code twice in one day (the Host had sent it at 08:21), four renters got first-timer onboarding they did not need, and — the sharpest edge — two sends walked past a human’s explicit Will Not Send (Caryl’s and Anita’s confirmations, WNS’d by hand Aug 24). Junyan held the lane: SWEEP_SAMEDAY_HOLD=1, revision event-gate-00072-ddm, 20:47Z.

    Two root causes, both gate defects. (1) “Same-day” tested when a booking starts, not when it was booked — so every regular whose weekly slot came around fell into the emergency lane. (2) The returning-renter gate matched Template = hta_studio_access, a field only the sweep fills in: the Host’s 141 access rows carried it 6 times, so the gate could see ~5% of real history and nearly every renter read as a first-timer. hta_queued() used the identical filter — two guards, one blind spot (the 845c93f3 disease: a gate reading only its own lane’s bookkeeping).

    The fix (dc-canon 6725b236, deployed 21:05Z, fingerprint 474134dedb13 verified byte-identical). One auto decision computed once per booking, three gates each independently sufficient to hold, and held always means Pending Review — never a silent skip: booked_late (last-minute = new in the between-pass snapshot’s made-list; Skedda carries no created-at; no snapshot = everything held), human_said_no (any WNS row for this renter+date holds the pair, matched by date stamp and written date forms, fails closed), and the master hold switch. prior_htas()/hta_queued() now match access texts by shape (_HTA_SHAPED: template OR title Access/HTA OR body “Door Code”), bringing all 427 queue rows into view. 7 new tests, 539 pass.

    State: the hold REMAINS ON — the gates are live but re-enabling auto-send is Junyan’s call, not a side effect of the deploy. This deploy also shipped two committed-but-undeployed changes from a sibling session: 2ee0c3a9 (/provision 409 is a retry) and 3b1c3563 (per-pass duration reporting, 087). Watch for: the first genuinely last-minute booking after re-enable — it should auto-send; a regular’s slot should not.

  • 2026-08-25 · 17:10Z

    A /provision 409 is a RETRY — the guard was in canon for two weeks and never in the codeDEFECT CLASS CLOSED

    The rule already existed; the implementation did not. the-loop.md has said since 08-10 that deleting an Alarm.com user never releases its code slot, so a code reading free in the Artist DB mirror can still be refused by an orphaned reservation — “That is a retry, not a lockout risk” — with the guard spelled out as “pick, write, read back … and on any refusal or mismatch re-pick.” booking_sweep.ensure_access implemented the pick and the read-back and not the re-pick: one 409 and it escalated to a human. The re-pick half lived only in prose.

    What it cost, concretely. Akira Huang booked and paid 509B for Fri 28 Aug at 22:55 on 08-23. Provisioning fired 9 minutes later, drew a held slot, took the 409 as terminal, and filed an Access/PIN Action row — which then sat unread for two days behind the disabled Concierge trigger. Retried by hand on 08-25, the first code tried was accepted. Nothing about it was specific to her: any renter drawing a reserved slot became a silent manual gate, and ghost reservations only accumulate, because deleting users never frees slots.

    The fix, and the carve-out that matters more than the fix. The lane now re-picks up to SWEEP_PROVISION_ATTEMPTS (default 5), choosing digits against the mirror and excluding what was already refused, then escalates naming every code tried — several different slots all held is a panel problem, not a collision. Re-pick ONLY when we are free to choose the digits. If the Artist page pins a code the renter may already hold those digits from an earlier message, so minting new ones behind their back is the Desiree Joy failure (08-06, a renter sent a code the panel did not hold) from the other direction; a refusal there stays a human's call. Non-409 errors are never retried — a 500 is the panel being broken, and retrying it just hides an outage. New AlarmHTTPError carries the status so 409 is told from 500 in code rather than by matching a message string. 530 tests pass, 5 of them new and each pinning one branch above.

    The two files that disagreed, reconciled. send-engine/SAMEDAY.md said “409 means access was written but does not cover the booking — the lane treats that as a stop”, and that is where the terminal reading came from. It describes the windowed call its own curl example passes window_start/window_end to — and windows were retired 2026-08-12. The callout three lines above it already said so, and also that allow_permanent is the expected verdict, contradicting the same paragraph's “pass is the only success”. Both lines struck and corrected. Note SameDay.gs itself is unchanged and still stops on any 409 — harmless while uninstalled, but it must be fixed before that lane ever goes live.

    Open and recorded rather than guessed: the panel's exact 409 semantics are still unread. alarm-mcp runs on Cloud Run and its source is not checked out anywhere on the Mac, so the collision reading is inferred — from the fact that Akira had no panel user at all after her 409, which the “access was written” reading cannot explain.     The Deck runs the engine’s preflight before offering Send — missing dispatch fields are fixable on the cardREVIEW SURFACE

    Every card now carries a Dispatch strip mirroring the Send Engine’s validate_(): SMS needs a valid phone, Email needs an address + subject + (for “Re:” subjects) Source Email. The boundary check in the engine protects the renter but costs hours; this puts the same check where the human is already looking, with the fix one input away.

    Junyan: “Why didn’t the review asks get autoqueued? Can we fix that?” The morning’s fix (25045c6d) forced the lane to query the How to Respond DB before composing — which converts a missed review ask into a matching problem. A match can be missed; a trigger cannot.
