  • 2026-08-25 · 05:30Z

    The mirror can now GIVE A ROOM BACK — a phantom hold is worse than an unheld roomNEW ROUTE + FAILURE MODE

    The gap. A booking site’s confirmation is not final — renters cancel, move and extend. The mirror lane written earlier today wrote rooms in ONE direction only, so the calendar would drift away from the platform and never drift back. A phantom hold is worse than an unheld room: an unheld room can still be sold, so the failure is a missed booking; a room held for a booking that no longer exists blocks a real renter silently, for as long as nobody looks, with no signal anywhere that it is wrong.

    How it surfaced — and a correction. I reported Leah’s Giggster booking (509A, 24 Aug) as a room we failed to hold. Wrong. She instant-booked at 18 Aug 00:56Z and cancelled 11 minutes later (01:07Z, “Cancelled by Renter”). The empty slot was correct; the mistake was reading an absence as a failure without checking what else the mailbox said about it. But the check exposed the real defect in the opposite direction: the lane would have held 509A at 00:56 and never let go, blocking that hour for six days.

    Shipped: POST /release-booking on skedda-mcp — deletes the mirrored booking and verifies by ABSENCE (a separate read that can no longer find it), idempotent so a re-delivered cancellation mail is harmless. Three guards, all server-side so a buggy or compromised caller cannot talk the server into deleting a real booking: (1) holder must be null — every platform mirror is casual with no Skedda user, so a holder proves the match is wrong, and this is what makes a wrong match survivable rather than destructive; (2) title must match exactly — for a holder-less booking the title is the only identity link there is; (3) never a recurring series, since cancelling a series id deletes every occurrence past and future. Extracted to release_refusal() and tested directly (8 tests) because it is the only destructive path on the server.

    Lane side: the cancellation mail names only a renter (“Your renter Leah”), so the lane now remembers what it holds and looks them up. It releases only when exactly one room is remembered; zero or more than one escalates rather than guessing. Detection is gated on the subject only — the body is unsafe, because a guest asking “what is the cancellation policy?” arrives as an ordinary message mail and Giggster’s own booking mails cite that phrase in their footer. Both traps are pinned as tests. 36 tests pass, including the full hold-then-cancel cycle on Leah’s real mail.

    Still open (ticket 111): booking CHANGES are not handled — Giggster “Booking update has been approved” is ignored, so a moved booking keeps its ORIGINAL slot (observed: Hanano’s 22 Aug booking moved 13:00→12:30). Peerspace/Tagvenue cancellation shapes are unverified. And /release-booking is written but NOT DEPLOYED — until ./deploy.sh runs it 404s, which escalates with an Actions row and an Ops Lead text naming the room to free by hand. That degradation is tested, so the lane is safe to enable before the deploy. Also corrected: pricing from platforms is always the payout (Junyan, 2026-08-24) — which settles Tagvenue, the one platform with no hand-made mirror to check against.

  • 2026-08-25 · 14:45Z

    The Registrar’s access lane now files ONE row per renter per day, and Message Codes name their studioFAILURE MODE

    Two defects from the 25 Aug registrar audit, both shipped in dc-canon 9d58a629 and confirmed live (/health → git_sha 9d58a629, fingerprint 0e552cf9dbe3, byte-identical to local; deployed 14:41:18Z alongside 108/090/110).

    Access rows merge across studios. The residue key was sweep:{studio}:{start}:access — one booking occurrence — so a renter holding four rooms for one evening drew four identical rows. Cara Butler: 509A + 509B + 527 + 901, one evening, four rows, all cancelled by hand. Access is a property of a person and a panel, not of a room: one code, one panel state, one text. The key is now sweep:access:{artist_id}:{date}, which merges the rooms and the times without merging the days or the renters. Same treatment applied to the same-day hta-owed row — one access text covers every room, so one row asks for it. The confirmation lane has always merged this way via merge_contiguous(); this is the lane that had no equivalent.

    Message Code carries the studio. BC-sweep-{MMDD-HHMM} collided the moment two renters booked different rooms in the same slot — Marielle Torrefranca (901) and Kristyan Calletor (509B) both carried BC-sweep-0823-1300. Now BC-sweep-0823-1300-901. The studio is a suffix by design: every reader in the codebase matches the code with a contains filter, so a prefix change would have blinded all of them at once. Old rows keep their old codes; nothing is rewritten.

    Watch for: a renter with several rooms on one day should now draw exactly one access row. Still open on this lane: the same-day branch gates on check.get("panels_covered") while check_access_covers returns systems_with_access — if the /provision verify_only response does not carry that exact key the condition is always false and every same-day booking files a gap regardless of the verdict. Flagged, not asserted; the response shape has not been read.

    4 new tests on the merge, 1 asserting the code suffix position; 516 pass repo-wide. Console: Is the Registrar Working? artifact carries the interactive version.

Re-enabled on Junyan’s approval and forced a catch-up run (cse_01L82SioaaNPZkADPNkzZ2jy), which created a live session; the schedule resumed at 2026-08-25T16:10Z.

    The failure mode worth recording is that everything downstream stayed green. The overnight reports kept arriving — 6/6 in, all five earlier “missing report” system exceptions resolved themselves — and the security lane genuinely was clear, so the Morning Text read “Overnight clear” on all three mornings. It was telling the truth about the alarm panels and saying nothing at all about the desk. Nothing consumed those reports, because consumption is INTAKE-FIRST inside the Concierge pass and there was no pass. Reports arriving is not work being done, and no monitor was watching the gap between the two.

    What it cost: Actions backlog reached 84 open at 14:20Z (65 Ops Lead, 16 Junyan, 3 Owner) against the console’s 80 at 08:40Z — roughly 4 rows per two hours of downtime, still climbing while unread. 28 open tickets, oldest 52 days. The Night Watch itself behaved correctly throughout: no NW-HANDOFF-V1 marker meant it rendered Concierge handoff incomplete and refused to relabel raw overnight findings as Ops Lead work, which is exactly the Step 5 contract. The console was right and the red banner was the signal; it just had no one reading it for three days.

    Gap left open: nothing alerts on a fleet trigger that is disabled rather than failing. A failed run leaves a session with a log; a disabled trigger leaves no row in list_runs at all, so absence of runs is indistinguishable from a quiet night. A cheap watch would compare each RUNTIMES.md trigger’s last_fired_at against its own cadence and shout when the gap exceeds two intervals — the “silence is not the same as clear” class this outage belongs to.

    Also surfaced, and the reason it stayed broken is its own defect: Akira Huang had no panel identity at all for booking 119552777 (509B, Fri 28 Aug 14:00–15:30, paid, created 08-23 during the blackout). The interesting part is not that provisioning was missing — it is that it ran on time and was refused. The Doorman’s Action row carried Basis = “/provision HTTP 409” stamped ~9 minutes after the booking. Per the-loop.md, deleting an Alarm.com user does not release its code reservation, so a code that reads free in the Artist DB mirror can still be refused by an orphaned slot — canon calls that “a retry, not a lockout risk” and prescribes “pick, write, read back … and on refusal pick again.” The lane picked once, got 409, stopped, and escalated to a human. send-engine/SAMEDAY.md documents 409 as a hard stop for its lane, which is where the terminal reading comes from. So a self-recoverable condition became a human gate — which then sat unread for two days behind the disabled trigger. Resolved 08-25 attended, on the first retry: alarm user 248327893, code 8291, Existing + Future across 4 panels, no window (permanent-until-removed); check_access_covers → allow_permanent.

That reading of a first-timer is exactly what ticket 108 fixes by narrowing the rule to known-artist-only, and 108 is built with tests passing but still not deployed. It has a named precedent: Brandy Honore Pierre, 2026-07-06, recorded in fast-track.md — a self-completed booking dismissed with no ticket, so no board row existed and her PBF never fired.     The platform mirror caller is BUILT — parsers pinned to real mail, which caught three silent-failure bugsNEW LANE + FAILURE MODE

    Junyan ruled: “Once the site confirms, a person doesn’t do it.” No human step between a Peerspace / Giggster / Tagvenue confirmation and the room being held. send-engine/PlatformMirror.gs implements exactly that and nothing more: parse the mail → map listing → studio through the 🏢 Studio Reference DB → call POST /mirror-booking with confirm:true → treat anything short of verified as failure. It provisions no access, creates no cards, sends nothing, and attaches no Skedda holder. Standalone — no dependency on SameDay.gs, which can now be deleted. 28 tests pass (node test_platform_mirror.js); setup in send-engine/PLATFORM-MIRROR.md. Not yet enabled — Script Properties + trigger are Junyan’s to switch on.

    The parsers are pinned to REAL captured mail, and that is the whole point. The previous generation was written against the Gmail API’s pipe-delimited rendering and silently matched nothing on GmailApp.getPlainBody(). Three live bugs fell out on the first replay, each of which meant a silently unheld room: Julia’s Peerspace mail has no “Space” label (the listing is the line after “Booking details”); Peerspace separates date and time with a literal <br/>, not a newline; and Peerspace indents its labels, so a ^Payout anchor never matched. The failure signature of all three is silence, never an error.

    Price is the PAYOUT, settled empirically rather than by preference — four bookings Junyan mirrored by hand all carry the payout, not the gross: Julia 64, Kim M. 117.60 (gross was 147.00), Chris Giggster 145.80 (headline was 180), Jessica T. 48. The Giggster parser had been reading the headline and is changed. Tagvenue stays UNVERIFIED — no hand-made Tagvenue mirror existed to check against — and is flagged in code and doc rather than quietly assumed.

    Two live findings. (1) Leah’s Giggster booking was never mirrored. RETRACTED same day — she instant-booked 509A at 00:56Z on 18 Aug and CANCELLED 11 minutes later (01:07Z). The empty slot was correct; the error was reading an absence as a failure without checking what else the mailbox said. It surfaced a real gap in the opposite direction — see the entry above. (2) The 🏢 Studio Reference row for 693 was stale, saying “Contemporary Downtown Fitness & Event Studio” where Peerspace says “Dance”; Kim M. would have escalated instead of mapping. Corrected in Notion and verified by read-back, with a test pinning that a one-word drift must escalate rather than fuzzy-match — guessing a room is worse than not holding one.

    Known gap, recorded not hidden: Giggster “Booking update has been approved” (a renter moving or extending) is ignored, so a moved booking drifts from the calendar until someone notices. That needs an update path rather than a create path and belongs with ticket 084. Also pinned as negative tests: Giggster Wrap Reports and “Get ready” mails carry an identical renter/listing/date block to a real booking — mirroring one would re-book a past slot.

Inside the window the sweep now runs its full chain — artist record, panel-verified access (on first sight, per 110), board row, queued confirmation — and files a keyed hta-owed Actions row for the ONE human step, sending the access text. No send authority moved; the only unattended code-send grant stays in the uninstalled SameDay.gs until 089 retires it. New kill switch SWEEP_SAMEDAY_DISABLED=1 reverts this branch alone, so undoing 108 no longer costs the whole sweep. The misleading :sameday-gap row (it told readers to “check SameDay.gs ran”) is gone with the branch.     2. The decisive finding: the SameDay parsers could not have been reused — they were written against the Gmail API’s pipe-delimited rendering, and against GmailApp.getPlainBody() they match nothing (Julia’s real confirmation has no “Space” label at all).     The acceptance is the trigger (Junyan, 08-25): “this acceptance should have automatically set everything up immediately.” The confirmation mail exists BECAUSE the host accepted, so the mail-fired caller fires on the acceptance itself; the sweep’s next pass then does card + code + row off the mirrored booking’s title. One tap at 1:50am leaves nothing owed by morning except messages humans send by rule.

  • 2026-08-25 · 03:05Z

    The trouble classifier is inverted — an unnamed panel condition now files a ticket instead of vanishingDEFECT CLASS CLOSED

    The root cause behind the six-day tamper, fixed at the class level rather than one condition at a time. escalating_troubles and ticketable_troubles were pure allowlists. A panel condition on neither list, and not on the ignore list either, reached the end of the function and was discarded — no error, no log line, no row. That is not an alert that failed; it is an alert that could not exist. 509B reported Panel Tamper from 2026-08-18 to 2026-08-24 and nothing reported it, because nobody had ever typed the words “Panel Tamper” into a tuple.

    One classify() now owns the policy, and its DEFAULT is unknown → file a ticket. Buckets in order: ignored (checked first, so silencing something actually silences it) → critical (SMS) → handled (another module owns it, e.g. tamper.py) → ticket → unknown. Both original selectors were rewritten to route through it, so there is exactly one place the policy lives.

    A ticket and never a text, deliberately. A condition we cannot classify is by definition one we cannot call urgent, and an SMS for something that turns out to be routine is how a person learns to ignore the next SMS. The row is quiet, deduped to once per 24h while the condition stays open, and its body says how to close it out permanently — add the phrase to IGNORED_PHRASES / TICKET_PHRASES / CRITICAL_PHRASES, or give it its own module. A junk row costs one decision instead of recurring forever.

    Two consequences worth knowing. (1) IGNORED_PHRASES is now load-bearing: it used to be a courtesy, since unrecognised conditions were dropped anyway — it is now the ONLY thing that silences a condition, so adding to it is a real decision. (2) TAMPER_PHRASES moved to escalate.py and tamper.py imports it; two copies would drift and the first symptom would be every tamper filing two tickets (pinned by test_tamper_is_not_double_filed).

    Checked against the live panel before shipping: the four Credentials In Conflict open since 2026-07-10 classify as ignored, so this deployed with zero new rows. 15 tests; 243 pass. Image alarm:classifier-0825, revision alarm-mcp-00053-2rk, verified quiet across the 03:00Z bypass sweep. Expect a few junk rows over the coming weeks as the panels emit things nobody has named — that is the mechanism working, and each one is one decision away from never appearing again.

    Panel Tamper went unreported for six days — now a closed loop: ticket → ask Stefan → auto-resolveDEFECT + NEW LANE

    Studio 509B reported Panel Tamper from 2026-08-18 19:50Z and nothing said a word until Junyan spotted it on 08-24. Not an alert that failed — an alert that could not exist. escalate.py matches panel troubles against allowlists (CRITICAL_PHRASES → SMS, TICKET_PHRASES → ticket), and “Panel Tamper” was on neither. It was not in IGNORED_PHRASES either, so nobody had ever decided to ignore it — it fell off the end of the function. Junyan texted Stefan 10:11 Toronto 08-24; Stefan rebooted and found the panel “partially unclipped from the base” by 14:11; cleared by 08-25 02:27Z.

    New lane — alarm_monitor/tamper.py, on the existing every-60s watchdog tick (no new schedule, no new credential beyond a phone number). Runs regardless of WATCHDOG_ARMED: it sends no panel command, it asks a person to walk over. Three limits, Junyan's: (1) ticket filed immediately at any hour — only the SMS waits; (2) Stefan is texted 08:00–20:00 Toronto, at most once per calendar day, so a tamper flapping all afternoon is still one text; (3) two asks per episode, then it stops asking Stefan and texts the on-call phone instead — Stefan's own read on 08-24 was that the panel is “kind of broken. No idea how to repair it fully without replacing it”, so a reboot that will not hold is a fact about the hardware and the third message belongs with whoever can authorise a replacement.

    One rule Junyan did not ask for, flagged as such: CHRONIC_EPISODES (3 episodes / 14 days) stops the asks across episodes. Each clear resets the per-episode counter, so without it a failing panel earns one fresh “please reboot” every day forever and the two-ask cap in (3) is never once reached — the exact nuisance that cap exists to prevent, arriving by the back door.

    On clear the row is set Resolved with the clear time and how long it was open; an escalated tamper is deliberately left OPEN and Urgent, since a panel awaiting a replacement decision is not resolved. send_sms now takes an explicit recipient, defaulting to ON_CALL_PHONE so every existing caller is unchanged. Episodes and ticket ids ride the same GCS ledger as the trip/bypass/alert state — a cold start must not re-file a ticket or re-text Stefan for a tamper already being handled. 17 tests; 228 pass. Image alarm:tamper-0825, revision alarm-mcp-00050-wmf.

    The sweep gains a memory — one-request board read, made/changed/cancelled, and door access due on BOOKING rather than on the horizonROUTE CHANGE + CADENCE + FAILURE MODE

    Three changes to the Registrar, built and tested, NOT deployed. dc-canon 438d57ca and aaa2e2d4; 415 tests pass (test_app_relay/test_classifier/test_poller need fastapi, absent locally — pre-existing, untouched).

    1. The board read is one request, however wide (ticket 090 steps 1–2). skedda_read.bookings_range(first, last), both ends inclusive; bookings_on(day) is now a thin wrapper over it and presence/loop are untouched. Until now read_board() looped per day, so a 14-day horizon cost 14 round trips to /bookingslists and a 45-day one was unaffordable. The span is now free, so the horizon becomes a policy choice rather than a budget. SWEEP_HORIZON_DAYS default deliberately left at 14 — the 45-day flip is a config change Junyan makes on the service, reversible in seconds, not a code deploy. Thirteen sweep tests that monkeypatched bookings_on were moved to bookings_range: left alone they would have stopped intercepting and attempted real HTTP, a silent hole in the suite rather than a failure.

    2. The sweep now remembers the previous pass (090 step 3). New sweep_snapshot.py holds one JSON object in GCS through the JSON API and the instance metadata-server token — no new dependency; this service still ships fastapi/uvicorn/pytest and nothing else. diff_board() reports made / changed / cancelled. This is a deliberate, narrow exception to the sweep’s no-stored-state design, and what it holds is a cache of reality, not a processed-set. New failure mode closed by design: a missing or unreadable snapshot returns known:false with EMPTY lists — never “everything is new”, which would re-announce the whole board and, under 110 below, hit the alarm panel once per booking on it. None (no store) and {} (a pass that saw nothing) stay distinguishable, because against a genuinely empty board every booking IS new. A 403 is raised, not swallowed as “nothing stored yet” — a revoked permission must not read as a quiet day. Every pass reports its own snapshot state in the run summary for the same reason.

    3. A cancellation inside the board window now raises a Review row. Previously nothing noticed. A booking that vanishes from the board while it still lies ahead, and near enough that the sweep had already built for it, files sweep:cancelled:<id> naming the FBS row and the Message Queue. The point: a queued confirmation for a cancelled booking must not be released. A booking that merely finished is not a cancellation and raises nothing; a far-off cancellation raises nothing either, since nothing was built to clean up. The alarm code is deliberately left alone — codes are permanent and the renter may return.

    4. Door access is due on BOOKING, not on the horizon (ticket 110, Junyan ruling 2026-08-24). “I want to provision door access the moment someone makes a booking (it’s not based on the horizon).” Until now access was due as a side effect of the read window — you got your code when the horizon reached you, which nobody ever decided. Now a booking absent from the snapshot gets its access step on that pass whatever its start date, and a booking already in it is not re-checked. That second half is what makes the first affordable: ensure_access costs an _alarm_call({verify_only}) plus two Notion reads per booking per pass, so a wide horizon without a first-sight gate would grind the panel over settled bookings forever. Safe because codes are permanent since windows were retired (doorman-access-lifecycle.md), and because the Doorman’s T−7 preflight remains the net for a code that rots between booking and session — that is the job that owns arrival. With no snapshot, access falls back to exactly the pre-110 behaviour.

    090 re-scoped, and 084 closed. Junyan, 2026-08-24: “I don’t have to read any emails anymore as it all goes through the desk and works pretty well.” 090 was justified as a REPLACEMENT for reading the Gmail label; that need is met, so the 📥 Bookings tab is no longer the deliverable. What survives is load-bearing in a way the tab never was: cancel detection is a correctness guard, and first-sight is now a dependency of 110. Separately, ticket 084 (platform bookings beyond same-day) is closed — all four questions answered, two by observation against live records (~20 Peerspace guests already hold cards with verified codes; the FBS board finds them by booking TITLE, not by holder, which is why blank-holder mirrors work at all) and two by ruling: platform bookings stay casual with no Skedda user, and the title convention is <Name> <Platform> (e.g. Kim M. Peerspace). That title is the ONLY identity link a platform booking has, so sdMirrorSkeddaBooking_’s <name> (<Platform>) form must change before 109 ships or it introduces a fourth variant.

    Still owed by Junyan, in order: (1) create the GCS bucket and bind the event-gate service account, then set SWEEP_SNAPSHOT_BUCKET — until then the diff and 110 are inert by design and the sweep behaves exactly as it does today; (2) deploy event-gate; (3) optionally raise SWEEP_HORIZON_DAYS, which is what turns “access on booking” from a 14-day promise into a real one. Paper trail: dc-canon 438d57ca, aaa2e2d4; tickets 084 (closed), 090, 110 on disk and on the tickets board.

    The mirror’s bearer is settled — and confirm:true is ruled a machine interlock, not a human approvalCREDENTIAL + RULING

    The credential. POST /mirror-booking on skedda-mcp (revision skedda-mcp-00043-6j4) has been deployed and idempotent since 08-12, gated on a bearer. There is no new secret to mint: it is the SAME shared mcp-auth-token (Secret Manager, project danceannex-skedda, 64 chars) that event-gate already mounts as ALARM_PANEL_TOKEN. Verified live this session — unauthenticated 401, authenticated 400 missing required field(s): studio, start, end, title against a deliberately empty body, so the probe proved auth while writing nothing. Ticket 109’s stale revision reference (skedda-mcp-00020-bxk) corrected in the same pass.

    The ruling (Junyan, 2026-08-24): every Skedda write is a dry run until the call carries confirm:true. That flag is an interlock against ACCIDENTAL writes — a preview call, a retried read, a half-built payload — and is not a request for a person’s permission. An LLM agent, a deterministic caller, or a human may set it; all three are equal. Agents must not stall a booking waiting for Junyan to confirm a routine calendar write. What still needs a human is unchanged and is a different category — message-sends, not writes: create_user (Skedda emails the guest a registration invite on commit), create_invite_link, and cancelling a booking a renter already holds.

    Why it matters, on the record: Julia S.’s Peerspace booking (Sun 23 Aug, 509A) reached the calendar a day after her session ended, because the only thing that could hold the room was a human at the desk — 509A sat free and double-bookable throughout. A room left unheld can be double-booked; a room held wrongly is released in one call. The asymmetry favours writing.

    Paper trail: dc-canon b4fe97ad (new § in library/skedda-session.md, mirrors to Notion on the next 30-min tick), skedda-cli c19737b (README no longer teaches “confirm the prompt”), ticket 109 on disk. Still open: mounting SKEDDA_HTTP_URL + SKEDDA_HTTP_TOKEN wherever the 109 caller runs — a Cloud Run env merge Junyan must run, since a production deploy is outside this session’s grant.

  • 2026-08-22 · 00:20Z

    The Responder decides whether to reply before deciding what to say — silence is a valid outputROUTE CHANGE

    Junyan directive: “Angus has already been responded to enough. A responder should look at the full context before proposing a courtesy reply.” The situational-courtesy change earlier the same day (1be9019a) capped the lane at one reply per artist per pass and superseded standing held rows — but it never taught the lane to read how much has already been said and choose to stay quiet. It would still have proposed a warm ack to someone we had already thanked repeatedly, just one at a time instead of three. Silence was not an available answer.

    New gate, ahead of composing (canon a535499b). Propose nothing when: we already had the last word and the inbound after it adds no question (a bare “Thank you!” closing an exchange we already closed); the exchange has reached its natural tail (thanks → you’re welcome → thanks again is done); sent acks to this person are stacking up; or two or more of their recent courtesy rows were closed Will Not Send — the desk stating plainly that it does not reply to this person this way.

    Why the last one matters most: Angus Dirnbeck reached seven consecutive killed acks before anyone noticed the pattern, and every single one was individually defensible — which is exactly why no per-row check ever caught it. The signal only exists across rows.

    Silence is countable, never a silent no-op: logged as — <artist> · skipped: nothing left to say (<reason>). A pass that decided not to reply and a pass that never looked must not read the same on the board.

  • 2026-08-22 · 18:00Z

    The watchdog armed a studio on a person — occupancy guard added, watchdog re-armedDEFECT + FIX

    Studio 693, 12:20–12:25 Toronto. Hanano was inside. An alarm fired, she disarmed the panel at 12:20, and the watchdog disarmed and armed away on her at 12:22 and again at 12:24. She had to disarm three times in five minutes.

    Root cause: in_alarm stays LATCHED TRUE after a human disarm. executor.py’s pre-command recheck read in_alarm and nothing else, so her dealing with the alarm read as “siren still going, nobody has dealt with this”. It never asked whether the partition was still armed. The max_clears_per_alarm=2 cap added 08-18 is the only reason it stopped at two cycles — a cap meant for panel stubbornness ended up as the last thing standing between the system and a customer.

    Fix — _safe_to_cycle(), every check before the first command: (1) partition still in an armed state, checked FIRST and independently of in_alarm — a partition no longer armed was disarmed by somebody, the clearest presence signal this system can get; (2) still in_alarm; (3) no occupancy signs on that panel — any sensor open, or a motion detector reading anything but idle, scoped by panel-id prefix. Nothing runs between the disarm and the arm, because a refusal there leaves the studio open. Applies to bypass restores too — they also disarm/re-arm and could have done the same thing.

    Known limit: motion goes idle when a person stands still, so occupancy signs are a strong signal, not proof. The arm-state check is the reliable one.

    6 regression tests incl. test_a_human_disarm_stops_the_cycle_dead (with in_alarm deliberately still True) and test_every_check_happens_before_the_disarm; 211 pass. Image alarm:occupancy-0822. Watchdog switched OFF at 17:30Z during diagnosis, re-armed 18:00Z on Junyan’s approval (rev alarm-mcp-00047-vqd, armed=True confirmed in the tick log). Fourth time in eight days that production caught something recorded as settled — the pattern each time was treating a returned call as a changed world.

    Email-field ruling corrected: the card’s Email IS the send door — relay belongs there when no direct addressCORRECTION

    Junyan’s ruling, reversing yesterday’s “never in Email”. The Message Queue’s Email is a rollup off the Artist card, so a platform guest with a blank Email field can never receive an outbound email — the rule as written re-created the blank-card failure it was meant to end. First live case under the new pipeline, Kim M. [Peerspace] (card 10:03Z, relay fetched self-serve and filed in both places), was flagged as a violation and was in fact correct.

    Precedence now in canon (dc-canon commit d4064cb7, five pages): a real direct address always wins; otherwise the newest relay for the active enquiry sits in Email; never blank a populated field. Every relay is STILL a ☎️ Contact Points row — and since find_artist resolves people from Contact Points, a relay in Email can no longer mint duplicate cards, which was the fear behind the old rule.

    The Responder composes courtesy replies per situation — one per artist, not one per messageROUTE CHANGE

    Junyan directive: “What I actually would like is for the responder to assess the context and then make a response that matches the exact situation.” Two rules were producing canned, stacking acks, and both were working exactly as written — response-resolver.md step 4 made the lane a picker (HTR “Correct Response” VERBATIM, wording from there ONLY), and the-responder.md drafted per inbound message rather than per conversation.

    Trigger: Angus Dirnbeck accumulated three held acks across one Thursday — “Thank you 🙏”, “Anytime 🙏”, “No worries at all” — each matched to a different topic, so the Artist+topic dedup (step 4a) passed all three legitimately. Junyan had already answered him by hand; all three were killed Will Not Send, as were the previous four acks to the same renter. Coalescing alone would only have produced one canned ack; the ask was for a fitted one.

    What changed, courtesy class ONLY (canon 1be9019a): the drafting unit becomes the conversation — one artist, one pass, at most one reply covering everything unanswered since our last outbound; the class is composed, not picked, with HTR rows demoted to voice reference and Source HTR Row allowed blank with a justification; a standing held courtesy row is superseded, never accumulated.

    The bound that makes it safe — the no-new-facts guard: a composed courtesy reply may assert no fact that is not already on the record — no dates, prices, booking states, access or code details, availability, or promises. The moment a reply must state a fact it leaves the class and takes the verbatim path with every existing guard intact (context gate, Auto-send Criteria as a use precondition, response-shaped guard, temporal check). When in doubt, it is not courtesy class. The verbatim rule exists to stop a lane inventing policy, price or promises; on a message carrying none of those it bought no safety and cost the desk its voice.

    ⚠ Blind spot, stated not buried: there is no automatic check on courtesy wording — the drafted-vs-sent diff retired with Drafted Body (2026-07-29) and nothing replaced it. Loosening the wording rule makes reviewer edits the only quality signal on this class. The pass ledger now carries composed ✓ / no-new-facts ✓ / superseded <row> markers and logs courtesy-edited: <artist> when a released reply had its body changed; a recurring edit shape files an HTR suggestion.

  • 2026-08-21 · 13:40Z

    Desk Messages board: queue query could silently drop pending drafts — same LIMIT bug as The DeckFAILURE MODE

    The Desk Messages artifact’s Message Queue query had no ORDER BY with a LIMIT 200, while the queue lands ~330 Sent rows per 14 days — so the limit filled with send history and Pending Review/Error/Ready to Send rows could silently fall off the board. Same query family as the bug confirmed 2026-08-14 in the sibling “The Deck” artifact, fixed the same way: ORDER BY CASE WHEN Status is actionable THEN 0 ELSE 1 END, datetime("Created") DESC (keeping Ready to Send in the priority tier since this board shows releasing rows), sent-history window cut 14 → 7 days, LIMIT 250. Republished 2026-08-21 to the same artifact URL (4ccd5dad…).

    Why it matters: a draft dropped by this query is invisible on the one surface where drafts get reviewed and released — the failure reads as “nothing waiting” while a customer waits. Any future board querying the 📤 Message Queue must order actionable statuses ahead of the LIMIT; never rely on a bare LIMIT over a mixed live/history query.

  • 2026-08-21 · late

    A blocked tool call is not a capability limit — retry once before reporting impossibleFAILURE MODE

    Junyan caught this live, and he was right. In one fixer run, create_user, find_user_codes and scripts/gmail_reply_to.py were each reported to him as blocked. All three succeeded on a straight retry, same session, same arguments. The classifier denials were transient; the “impossible” was not real. One of them had already caused a live customer’s reply (Kim M., Peerspace) to be filed as undispatchable with a blank Reply To — the relay prod-6a8091…@reply.peerspace.com came back on the first retry.

    The rule now in canon (6e4344f5): before writing “blocked”, “not possible”, or “only Junyan can do this” — (1) retry the identical call once; (2) try the neighbouring surface (hosted connector vs local stdio, MCP tool vs repo script, API vs UI, file tool vs shell); (3) check the claim against the live tool list, because a row asserting “no robot can read this” is a claim about a tool surface and surfaces change; (4) only then report it, naming the exact call and the exact error. Also added to the /fixer skill’s 10-minute contract.

    Same day, same shape, from the other direction: a money row asserted the connector could not see card-on-file state and therefore no robot could settle it. The Stripe connector reads it in one call — card on file, and the $150 deposit had already settled on Aug 19. The row had been true when written and false ever since.

    Why it outranks convenience: a false impossible is worse than a failure, because it stops being retried. It gets written into a row, the row gets carried, and the carried row becomes the reason nobody looks again — exactly how four Responder prompt-paste items sat eleven days as “canon can’t reach the UI” when RemoteTrigger update had always written them over the API. This entry generalises that 2026-08-14 finding from stale claims to momentary ones.

  • 2026-08-21 · late

    Drafts land in the Message Queue — a mailbox draft is a mechanism, never the recordROUTE CHANGE

    Junyan directive. Any message an agent composes for human approval gets a 📤 Message Queue row at Pending Review — always, including replies composed inside a mailbox. A Gmail draft is a fine way to address a platform relay without knowing the relay address, but it is invisible to the board, carries no Reasoning, no Artist link and no audit trail. The row is where he looks; nothing waits for approval anywhere else. Canon dd6d8299.

    The row must carry its dispatch path. Not dispatchable by the Send Engine (blank Reply To on a relay, unreachable channel)? Say so in Reasoning and name what will actually send it. Two artefacts, one message — name the double-send risk out loud: send the mailbox draft → mark the row Sent; dispatch the row → delete the draft first. Never invent a recipient to make a row dispatchable.

    The case that forced it: Kim M. (Peerspace, 693, Thu Aug 27) booked on Aug 15 and went six days unanswered. Her reply was drafted correctly in Gmail because scripts/gmail_reply_to.py — the only reader of the relay address — was classifier-blocked, and Junyan could not see the draft anywhere. Row 3c37…4af9 now carries it. Her codes are deliberately NOT in that body: HOUSE-RULES 🌎 point 3 forbids codes through a platform relay (open defect 3b27…4e76 is the last breach) — they go by direct SMS once she supplies a number.

  • 2026-08-21 · 10:15Z

    Platform-ticket rule narrowed: bookings always, enquiries only while liveCORRECTION

    This morning’s rule was too broad, and a real case proved it within hours. Kiah F. booked 509A directly on Skedda on Aug 17, then her Giggster enquiry for the identical slot landed four hours later. The desk had handled it perfectly — one queued Giggster reply, “duplicate-slot inquiry, already booked”, no ticket — but the morning rule as written (“every enquiry or booking gets a ticket”) would have branded that correct behaviour a defect, and a verification pass judging by ticket-existence would have reported a false failure.

    The narrowed rule (dc-canon job-2-triage.md, relay-addresses.md, concierge-orchestration.md, commit a4304be7*): a platform booking always mints a ticket; an enquiry only while still live — check Skedda and the boards for the same person or slot first, and an already-satisfied enquiry gets a platform reply plus its relay filed, never a ticket. The relay contact point is owed on every platform contact either way. Proof of handling is the outcome surfaces (guest answered, card resolves from every contact point, no relay in Email, no duplicate card) — not ticket existence.

    *The edits ride a concurrent session’s commit — its git add -A swept this session’s in-progress files, third occurrence of that pattern. Content verified intact at origin/main.

    A new pipe: studio activity off the Alarm.com websocket — names, doors, second precisionNEW PIPE

    Replaces the dead ADT/TELUS email feed, and carries more than it ever did. New Cloud Run service alarm-events (revision alarm-events-00004-5qs, image 0820-ec3c101, the same image as alarm-mcp) holds one Alarm.com websocket open and writes door-events.json; alarm-mcp reads it via the studio_activity MCP tool and GET /door-history and never opens a socket. Canon: dc-canon/library/studio-activity.md — send agents there, not here.

    What it carries that the emails did not: the actor’s name and a stable contact_id (names drift, ids do not), plus door and motion events, live at second precision. Proven on live renters within the hour: Anneka K arming 509A, Angus Dirnbeck disarming 693, Jillia Cato-Weiler both directions at 901, Maggie Morrison at 509B. A back-to-back handover at 901 (Jillia out 22:14:06, Dana Brookman in 22:15:33) split correctly between two named people — the case the board could never resolve once ADT names were gone.

    One correction the wire forced: arming is not leaving. Anneka armed 509A then opened the door 12s later; Jillia’s gap was 5.5s. A departure taken from the arm event is systematically early, so the exit door event wins.

    The 2026-08-18 “history is locked to one selected system” finding still stands and must not be re-litigated: it only ever applied to the activity/historyEvents REST API. The websocket takes no unit parameter. Captured through the service’s own session, no browser, per Junyan’s ruling.

    Deploy shape is load-bearing: --min-instances=1 --max-instances=1 --no-cpu-throttling, all three. alarm-mcp is min-0, CPU-throttled between requests and maxScale 20, so a socket there would get no CPU, die, then run in twenty copies. Verified the always-on listener does not disturb the siren watchdog: 60s ticks, armed, quiet, zero errors throughout.

    New failure mode — a websocket has no replay. Events arriving during a disconnect are gone and cannot be queried back. Gaps are first-class ledger rows and every response carries covered. Never charge from a window where covered is false — route it to Review. Same ruling as the ADT retirement: a dead pipe must never read as renter fault. /arm-history is NOT retired and becomes the polling backstop, immune to these gaps; a departure present there but absent here is a gap, not a no-show.

    Doorman Job C stays suspended. The relay restores the names it needs but retains ~72h, and Job C asks a 30-day question — a 72-hour window answering it would retire every renter’s code. What is missing there is horizon, not identity.

  • 2026-08-20 · 22:22Z

    Three ways the new activity ledger claimed coverage it did not haveDEFECT + FIX

    Filed separately because the pattern is the lesson: every defect in this build was found by live data, and not one threw an error. The tests passed throughout. This integration fails by looking normal, so anything downstream must treat a clean-looking answer as a claim to check rather than a fact.

    1. Ten of 21 devices silently dropped. Sensor names do not follow the partition convention (527 Front Door, 693 Motion Detector, and one bare Front Door with no id at all), so the studio matcher lost both doors at 527 and 693. A dropped device produces no events, so the loss was invisible. Studio now resolves from the name including bare ids, falling back to the device’s own unit prefix — but only on single-studio panels, so the shared 509 panel stays unmapped rather than guessing between 509A and 509B.

    2. Gap detection depended on events that did not arrive. Observed live: 22:07:49 disconnected, three minutes of nothing, 22:11:15 events resume — no reconnect event, no gap recorded. The ledger claimed full coverage of a window the socket had provably been down inside. The flush loop now POLLS ws_controller.connected and reconciles; connection events remain only as an optimisation for precise boundaries. A gap found by poll is recorded wider than it truly was — deliberately the safe direction.

    3. A window from before the listener existed came back covered: true, basis: no_evidence — “we were listening and nobody was there” for a period nobody was listening to. A booking queried from before the service existed would have read as a clean renter no-show: the exact failure the ADT ruling exists to prevent, reproduced inside its replacement. Gaps describe outages between listens and say nothing about the time before the first. The ledger now carries covers_since, advancing with the 72h retention cutoff. Anything older than ~3 days now returns covered: false with no events, and that is correct — do not read an empty event list as “nobody was there”.

    Four ways to be uncovered, all failing closed: no ledger; window before covers_since; ledger not updated past the window end; a recorded gap overlapping. Unknown is never covered. Also fixed: motion detectors on these panels report Opened (15), never VideoAnalytics2Detection (302), so the device map — not the event code — decides the door/motion split.

    Insurance certificates get a pipe at last — Duuo intake lane, an Artist DB that can express “not yet in force”, and a third mailbox doorNEW LANE + SCHEMA + CONNECTOR

    What broke first. Enrico Casertano’s reissued certificate sat unread in the business mailbox for six days while his ticket waited at Gate: Artist “awaiting HUB” — an external wait parked on a dated revisit with nothing watching the mailbox for the reply. When it was finally read, the certificate was correct, but effective 2026-09-05 — and his 50-person Grand Opening is booked for Sept 4. The desk could not see this because the Artist DB recorded only Insurance Valid Until: a policy that had not started yet was indistinguishable from one in force. Owner accepted the Sept 4 exposure knowingly.

    The sweep that followed was worse. First-ever aggregate read of the insurance field found two Active Fixed Option renters reading as expired (Alison Murray since 2025-04-30, Anastasia Markevitch since 2025-11-22) — but Anastasia was a false alarm of the desk’s own making: she is a per-event renter whose certificate is supposed to lapse between events. There was no way to say that in the schema.

    Three fixes. (1) Artist DB gains Insurance Valid From (date) and Insurance Model (Annual policy / Per-event (Duuo) / Not required). Insurance Valid Until was deliberately not converted to a range — every stored value lives in the start slot, so flipping the semantics would have made every expiry read as a start date, and the effective dates to migrate with simply do not exist. (2) New local monthly task insurance-validity-check, cron 0 9 1 * * machine-local = 08:00 Toronto on the 1st (Mac on UTC-3). Classifies expired / expiring-60d / not-yet-effective / missing, skips per-event renters unless an upcoming booking has no cert, opens a PDF only in four named situations, and never contacts a renter. (3) New Apps Script lane DuuoTick.gs in the Send Engine project.

    The lane. Hourly duuoTick(), kill switch DUUO_ENABLED. Matches from:duuo@icsplatform.com + subject Proof of Event Insurance, files the COI-AF-*.pdf to ONE Drive folder, converts a temp copy to read effective/expiry/policy, and writes them onto the artist page. Filing and parsing are separate on purpose — a parser failure still files the certificate and links it, and only leaves the dates blank. Two guards worth knowing: it refuses to overwrite an Annual policy holder’s dates with a one-day event cert (that would manufacture the exact false alarm this lane exists to end), and a name matching more than one artist row files nothing and asks.

    Why Drive is the door. Duuo’s email says the renter adds us “as either an ‘additional insured’ OR a ‘co-host’” — different coverage, answer only inside the PDF, ~201 unopened emails. A cert that does not name us now raises a red Actions row. Certificates land in one Drive folder read through the hosted Drive connector, which behaves identically local and cloud. The rejected alternative was putting the business mailbox’s Gmail refresh token (scopes gmail.modify + gmail.settings.basic — send, delete, alter settings) into a cloud environment; Apps Script already runs as the mailbox owner, so nothing is delegated.

    ⚠ Third mailbox door, and CLAUDE.md’s two-connector warning now understates the trap. gmail-business (local stdio, @gongrzhe/server-gmail-autoauth-mcp, separate GMAIL_CREDENTIALS_PATH) now reaches thedanceannex@gmail.com and is the ONLY door with download_attachment — the hosted business connector has no such tool, which is why Enrico’s PDF was unreadable in-session. It is local-only: cloud triggers cannot reach a stdio server and must keep using the hosted connector. Scope it to interactive one-off reads; it is not a second general-purpose mailbox and not a lane.

    ⚠ Still open: Duuo offers free venue registration giving “an accurate Confirmation of Insurance with your venue automatically covered as an additional insured” plus alerts when a host buys or ends coverage — a cancellation signal the desk currently cannot see at all. Not done; owner’s call. Register both 527 and 509. Also open: DuuoTick.gs is written but not installed, and SameDay.gs is still uninstalled in the same project — two uninstalled lanes in flight.

    → Superseded 2026-08-21. DuuoTick.gs was deleted and rewritten as DocIntake.gs (two lanes — signed agreements and insurance — on one filing foundation), because this cut filed to an ad-hoc COI <artist> <date>.pdf name and created no 📎 Docs row: filing documents where the desk does not look. The Drive-vs-Sync folder question described in the 2026-08-21 entry also applies to everything written here. Kept as written; see the newer entry.

    What happened. First live run of the same-day access lane (12h catch-up window). Two HTAs auto-sent. Charlie Vernon James Foster was texted door + alarm codes for a booking Junyan had cancelled four minutes after creating it — the lane read the 12:44 “made a booking” email at 16:22 and never asked the calendar whether the booking still existed. Junyan sent both renters corrections by hand.

    Root cause, plainly: the lane treated the notification email as fact. The email is a claim about the past; the calendar is the present — the workspace’s own “verify against the live source before acting” rule, not applied to the very trigger of the lane.

    Three changes (SameDay.gs v7 + skedda-mcp verify-0820, revision skedda-mcp-00039-5lt):

    • Gate 0 — live verification. New read-only /find-booking route on the Skedda server (commit 46b5dae). Before provisioning anything, the lane requires the booking on the calendar NOW: cancelled → quiet skip + SMS, moved → the live time wins. Matcher is three-pass, strongest identity first (holder+time, holder, time-on-anonymous-row) — a bare time-match on a named row is refused, because Charlie’s cancelled 10:00 PM slot was reoccupied by Angus’s real 10:00 PM booking and a naive time-match would have verified the wrong renter. All four shapes replay-tested against the live payload.
    • Renter assessment. The queue row and SMS now state whether the Artist record matched (with prior-HTA count) or not — “NEW to our records” explicitly flags the Angus shape: possibly a regular under a name variant we do not hold.
    • Human gate. Rows now land at Pending Review, not Ready to Send; the send engine never touches them until a person flips the status. SAMEDAY_AUTOSEND=true restores auto-send when the lane has earned it back. Board HTA is only flipped on actual auto-send.

    Open residue: alarm codes 9700 (Charlie — no booking tonight) and 9951 (Angus — possible duplicate panel user next to his real record) are live and need an audit pass; the Angus duplicate Artist page wants a merge.

    The address card was wired to the auto-send lane and had never once firedFIX

    Junyan asked what the "Which address / building" card was actually for. The answer: three uses in fifteen days, zero sends, all three withdrawn. Every one was drafted by The Responder; the auto-send lane had never used it.

    The lane was wired correctly all along. cards.py points at that exact HTR row. The keyword pattern was written from the one hypothetical phrasing of the incident that prompted the card ("which address … the email says 527 Bloor") and matched none of the real questions: Hanano's "give me the exact address", Julia's "Am I in studio A or B?", Himanshi's "my booking today is at 693 and not 527". Himanshi's arrived by SMS on the desk line — the gate saw it and declined. Widened with negative cases asserted so it cannot creep into the codes, speaker, thermostat, won't-lock or locked-out lanes (dc-canon 3b8e4e90).

    Julia's case deliberately stays out. A which-studio card added 2026-08-16 already owns it and answers with the room plus the codes. A first draft of the widening swallowed its matches and broke its tests — that seam is now a comment in the code so the next person does not repeat it.

    The wording was the real defect, and Junyan rewrote it. The line "Always go by the address on your booking confirmation" was the card's original point, and it misfired on both in-scope uses: it pointed Julia at a Peerspace confirmation that does not show A vs B, and it corrected Himanshi — who was right, about our own text being wrong. Both drafts flagged it independently before being withdrawn. Replaced with a plain statement of where the renter is booked, plus a parking link.

    🚫 That link created a new rule. A booking platform's reply relay is always an EMAIL address, our website must never travel through one, and no lane can tell a renter's own address from a relay at drafting time — the queue row's Email is a rollup the send engine resolves later. So the exclusion is drawn one level wider than the risk: on email the URL line is stripped, every time; SMS is untouched. Whole lines, not just the URL ("details at ‹nothing›" reads as a broken promise), the original kept if stripping would empty the body, and the strip recorded in the row's Reasoning. Enforced in code (strip_urls_for_email, 58b9717d), in canon for the human and LLM lanes (RESPONSE-RESOLVER step 6, 4bcfcab5), and on the HTR row itself. Lane-wide, not card-specific — the rule is about email, not this one card.

    Cost, stated plainly: a renter with a real email and an international number loses the parking link. That is the price of not being able to see the address before writing the row. Live on event-gate-00058-5l4; 469 tests pass.

    Gaps filled: qa-review gets the same heartbeat, and The Mechanic—the fleet’s watcher—finally gets watchedGUARD

    qa-review. Same ticker as watchlist-refresh, same blind spot. It already files 📝 Run Error rows on failure, which covers runs that HAPPEN and go wrong — but not a run that never happens. Same run_heartbeat.py, roster row Hourly / stale 90 / window 00–24 (measured, not assumed: the last 30 runs cover every hour). The load-bearing detail is that the heartbeat fires on idle ticks: most ticks find no QA- rows and skip the expensive steps, so “nothing to do” is the normal healthy state and is exactly what must still prove liveness. Verified live on a 0-pending tick.

    The Mechanic (prompt v2). This one is the sharper finding. The cloud Mechanic had been writing a heartbeat report every single pass since it was created — but its prompt never set the Run Monitor relation, and The Sentinel builds its heartbeat map only from reports linked to a roster row. So the fleet’s watcher was writing an hourly heartbeat that nothing was reading. An unread heartbeat is the same as no heartbeat, and it looks identical to a working one from the inside — the exact failure shape this whole day has been about, reproduced one level up.

    v2 adds the relation (roster row Hourly / 90 / 00–24) and requires the report body to open with a 🕒 HH:MM — ok line, which is what The Sentinel’s §3a cross-check reads before it will believe any run is missing. v2 also tells the lane that a workflow which STOPPED FIRING is invisible to its email-only view, and to let the Run Monitor roster prove ticker liveness instead of trying to duplicate the check. Verified: the next run wrote both the relation and the 🕒 line; the two v1 reports show Run Monitor: null, which is the before-and-after in one query.

    Both rows were created Not reporting and flipped Live only after a real heartbeat landed — flipping first would have paged Junyan within the stale window for a lane that was fine.

    Known minor wart, recorded not hidden: the Mechanic’s §5 has no same-hour dedup, so two fires inside one clock hour create two identically-named report rows. Harmless (The Sentinel takes the newest) and impossible on the :54 cron — only manual re-fires, which is how it was seen.

    Paper trail: desk-correspondence .github/workflows/qa-review.yml, RemoteTrigger trig_01A2iWdZaRpqV4H5StkLKWoK prompt mechanic-cloud-v2-2026-08-20, RUNTIMES.md ticker section, 🚥 Run Monitor rows …8178b1a5fc9b8646be48 + …81cbb5a3d61a94ba83a4.

    The person lookup moves off one exact address and onto every contact point we holdLOOKUP ROUTE

    What moved. booking_sweep.find_artist’s email rung was Email exact-match on the 🎨 Artist Database. It is now “any row in the new ☎️ Contact Points table whose Value matches”. The Skedda User ID rung is unchanged and still first; the name rung is unchanged, still last, and still deliberately ambiguous. The old Email query survives as a fallback for a card created between the back-fill and its first contact-point write, and decays on its own because every card the sweep now creates writes its contact points at creation.

    Why. Tagvenue, Peerspace and Giggster mint a fresh reply-relay per enquiry, and it lands in the card’s Email field — so every repeat booking from the same human minted a NEW card. Unbounded, unlike the bounded private-vs-group duplicate class. 42 cards had a relay as their primary address; 25 names held more than one card.

    New data source: cbf5b8aa-f836-4fee-aa1f-d0da48c4fda2 (☎️ Contact Points), one row per address or number, related to the Artist. Back-filled to 1,709 rows over 1,051 cards by dc-canon/scripts/contact_points_backfill.py — additive only, re-runnable, and it reports what it cannot normalize rather than guessing. Coverage verified twice (own re-read + an independent Notion aggregate), per this workspace’s standing warning that one query is never proof.

    New failure mode to know about: normalization is now load-bearing. The Value is the key, so emails are stored and looked up lowercased and phones in E.164. A case or format mismatch between writer and reader silently reintroduces the exact duplicate-card class this replaced — it would look like “person not found”, which the sweep answers by creating a card. Any new writer into this table must normalize the same way.

    New side effect on the SMS lane. A Giggster guest-chat text now files its own Contact Points row from app.parse_giggster_relay — proxy number as the value, conversation uuid as the durable key, and no Artist attached, because a display name like “Hanano I.” is not an identity. Best-effort: an inbound SMS is never failed because Notion was slow. Transactional Giggster notices mint nothing. Feeds wayfinder ticket 102 (proxy recycling).

    Retired: Known Emails. Nothing ever searched it, so the standing habit of parking a second address there recorded a fact and fixed nothing. Renamed on the card to Known Emails (retired → Contact Points).

    NOT yet live. The lookup is committed (dc-canon 22b20096, suite green at 463) but event-gate has not been redeployed, so the scheduled booking-sweep is still matching on Email alone. A local dry-run against the live board on the new code was clean — 162 scanned, 0 errors, no new residue, one correctly-ambiguous card — so what remains is the deploy, in ticket 082’s order: deploy → dry-run read → unattended.

    Paper trail: wayfinder concierge-repair tickets 098 (shape), 101 (build), 103 (deploy).

  • 2026-08-20 · 18:09Z

    watchlist-refresh gets a heartbeat: a stopped ticker is now detectable, because no error channel can see oneGUARD

    On 2026-08-20 watchlist-refresh went dark for 9h15m (02:45Z→12:00Z) when its cron-job.org dispatcher stopped, and nothing noticed. Nothing could: a workflow that never starts produces no run, no failure and no email — silence and health look identical. Removing the GitHub schedule: crons on 08-18 (to stop double-billing) had made that single ticker the only thing standing between the desk and a silent stop.

    What was added: scripts/run_heartbeat.py, the workflow’s LAST step under if: success(). It keeps one rolling daily 📊 Workflow Report (appending the 🕒 HH:MM body lines The Sentinel’s §3a cross-check reads) and stamps Self-heartbeat on a new 🚥 Run Monitor row — Cadence 15-min, Stale after 45, window 08–23 Toronto. No new watchdog was built: The Sentinel already handles 15-min rows, including the max(hb, today’s Window start) rule that stops the overnight gap reading as staleness.

    Two design points that carry the weight. The step sits after the deferred Phone.com re-raise, so success() is false whenever anything failed — one signal covers both “the ticker stopped” and “every run is failing”. And the heartbeat could NOT be inferred from the job’s own output: watchlist_build.py is idempotent and writes nothing when nothing changed, so a healthy quiet run leaves no trace. Freshness of output is only a heartbeat when the writer is unconditional.

    Bug found and fixed during the build: the first live run failed with a bare HTTP Error 400 — the script pinned Notion-Version: 2022-06-28, on which the /data_sources/… endpoints do not exist. It is diagnosable only because the step is non-fatal and logged; the catch-all was then rewritten to read Notion’s response body into the message and to stop retrying 4xx, since a malformed request is our bug. Verified end to end: 3 local runs produced ONE report with 3 timestamped lines, then a real CI run wrote 🕒 14:09 — ok. Row flipped Live only after that.

    Still blind: qa-review rides the same ticker with the same gap. run_heartbeat.py takes the run name and roster id as arguments so adopting it is one step plus one roster row.

    Paper trail: desk-correspondence scripts/run_heartbeat.py + .github/workflows/watchlist.yml, RUNTIMES.md ticker section, 🚥 Run Monitor row 3c275032-81c4-8100-b634-f318e3158cfe.

    Same-day access lane: a two-room booking now names both rooms — and refuses to send if either walkthrough is missingFAILURE MODE

    Found pre-flight, before the lane was ever enabled. sdNormalizeStudio_ in send-engine/SameDay.gs took the first studio it matched in a Skedda Spaces line, so a booking held in two rooms — Studio 509A, Studio 509B, as Vanessa Zavatti’s 2026-08-20 1:00 PM booking was — collapsed to 509A. Verified before shipping that all five studios have sent HTAs carrying a youtu link (509A 8, 509B 9, 527 7, 693 4, 901 6) and that 509A/509B use different videos, so this guard will not fire spuriously.
    • [plural if needed] is tied to the number of walkthrough links, not the number of rooms. The canonical wording pluralises “video” (walkthrough[plural if needed], watched the video[plural if needed]) — keying it to room count would say “videos” above a single link.

    Platform bookings are unaffected: a Giggster/Peerspace/Tagvenue listing maps to exactly one studio, and sdStudioList_ falls back to [booking.studio] when no list is present. Confirmation SMS and escalation headlines now read Studios 509A + 509B.

    Held QA replies get a surface — a content hold now files an Actions row the fixer walk readsNEW ROUTE

    Closes the open item from the entry below. When the hourly qa-review sweep holds a QA- row because a review check failed — wrong card, name mismatch, unfilled placeholder, emergency language, codes outside the codes card — it now also files one row in the ✅ Actions to Perform DB: Held QA reply — <renter> — <topic>, Type Other, For Ops Lead, with the failed check, the inbound text and the queue row id in Basis. The daily fixer walk reads that board, so a hold reaches a human the next morning with the reasoning attached, instead of living in a Notion comment nothing surfaces.

    Content holds only, by design. An infrastructure failure — API error, race lost, token trouble — files nothing: those self-clear on the next tick and already have the workflow’s Run Error path. Filing them would bury the real holds in noise, which is the disease this route treats. Dedup: one open row per renter per held reply; a repeat hold appends to Basis rather than duplicating.

    Prompt-only change (scripts/qa_review_prompt.md, dc-canon e7ac4f7d); the workflow reads it from checkout, so the next hourly tick carries it — no deploy. Row shape proven live by cards.file_actions_row against the same data source.     Send Engine v19: Invariant K — proof of dispatch. Every send stamps a provider message id into the new Dispatch Receipt columnGUARD

    The Samara (Jul 28) and Tess/David (Jul 21) incidents shared one shape: a row claiming Sent with no message actually delivered, invisible until a renter complained. v19 closes the class at the source instead of auditing after the fact: both send paths now return the provider’s message id (gmail:<id> / phonecom:<id>) and the engine stamps it into the Message Queue’s new Dispatch Receipt property immediately after dispatch. Fresh emails move from GmailApp.sendEmail (returns nothing) to the Gmail API + buildMime_ path the reply branch has used since v12.

    Failure semantics: Gmail API resolving without an id → throws → row flips to Error (loud). Receipt stamp failing after a real send → row STAYS Sent with a row comment + alert email — flipping to Error post-dispatch invites a re-release and a double send. Phone.com 2xx with unparseable body → marker receipt phonecom:http-2xx, never an Error flip.

    Watcher: The Mechanic’s hourly sweep (J13 lane A) gained a receipt check: Status=Sent, Sent At >15 min old, empty receipt, SMS/Email lanes only → claimed-but-not-dispatched, filed to Actions to Perform.   • 2026-08-18 · 21:0xZ

    The Phone.com rotation runbook’s cold-start command was a no-op, so every rotation updated the stores and left the connector serving the dead secretFIX

    Symptom: phonecom-mcp tools returned Phone.com API 401: token invalid/revoked while the token in ~/.env authenticated against api.phone.com/v4/accounts with HTTP 200. The credential was healthy the entire time. A rotation had already been done that afternoon — Secret Manager version 2 was created 14:17Z and an SMS at 14:22Z confirmed the new token worked — yet the connector was still 401ing at 20:00Z.

    Cause: canon’s §3 runbook ended with a bare gcloud run services update phonecom-mcp --region … with no flags. That exits ERROR: (gcloud.run.services.update) No configuration change requested and mints no revision. The secret is bound as secretKeyRef: key: latest, so a new version is only picked up by a new revision — the service went on serving version 1, which had been disabled. Three of the four stores were current and the one that serves traffic was not.

    Fix: the command now carries a churn value — --update-env-vars COLD_START=$(date +%Y-%m-%dT%H%M) — which forces a revision without changing behaviour. Rotation completed across all four stores: ~/.env, GitHub Actions secret, Secret Manager version 3, and Cloud Run revision phonecom-mcp-00007-5n7. list_sms verified live afterwards. Canon patched at CREDENTIALS.md §3, commit f18020d3.

    The second rule added to canon: verify before rotating. The instinct on a 401 is to mint a new token, and it is usually wrong — curl the API with the existing one first. Rotating a working credential turns a one-place problem into a four-place problem, and the panel’s regenerate button issues short-lived tokens that expire within hours (the cause of the 2026-07-17 SMS outage), so a reflex rotation can also seed the next failure.

    Also found, unfixed: list_sms accepts direction: "in" and returns outbound messages anyway — the filter is not applied server-side. Anything relying on it is reading the wrong set. Not blocking, but it silently widens every caller’s result.

    Why this class of failure hides: each store reports success on its own write, and nothing compares what the running revision is actually serving against what the stores hold. The 401 is loud, but it points at the credential — the last place worth looking.

  • 2026-08-20 · 02:14Z

    The Registrar’s first unattended run was killed by a timeout with no margin in itINCIDENT

    Switched on at 01:34Z; first scheduled pass at 02:00Z returned 504 DEADLINE_EXCEEDED after 299.97 seconds. Cloud Run’s request timeout and the Cloud Scheduler attempt deadline were both set to 300s, so there was no slack anywhere — the first limit it hit was also the last. The dry passes used to bless the release ran 146s and 239s and looked like comfortable margin; they were not. A live pass writes, so it is slower than a dry one, and it went over.

    Both raised to 900s (event-gate-00049-dc4; --attempt-deadline=900s), verified afterwards — still 19 env variables, nothing dropped. The killed pass had written partially, which is harmless here: every write is idempotent and fill-only, and the next complete pass re-derives the whole board from scratch. Nothing wrong was written; it was incomplete, not corrupt. No messages went out — confirmations queue at Pending Review regardless.

    900s is a tuning fix, not a repair. The sweep reads the board one day at a time — 15 sequential round-trips across the 14-day horizon before it touches a single booking — so duration scales with the board and the board grows. The true ceiling is 1800s no matter what the timeout says, because the job runs every 30 minutes: past that, passes overlap, and overlap breaks single-writer, the constraint 079 learned from the Codex collision (two actors on one instruction, each verifying its own write as correct, nothing detecting it). Ticket 087 carries the real fix: ask for a date range, which /bookingslists already accepts.

    Considered and declined: shrinking the horizon to 7 days would have halved the reads tonight, but bookings 8–14 days out would then get no prerequisites until they drifted closer. That is seeing less, not going faster.

    The lesson worth keeping: two limits set to the same number is not two safety nets, it is one. And a dry run is not a timing test for a lane that writes.

  • 2026-08-20 · 03:0xZ

    The Gmail ledger poller only ever looked forward, so any message it missed once was missed forever — it now re-scans a week, dailyFIX

    The failure mode, stated plainly: gmail_pull.py lists with after:{cursor - 3600} and then advances the cursor past everything it saw. A message that was invisible at poll time — quarantined in spam is the leading candidate — is skipped, and the skip is permanent: a human clicking “Not spam” an hour later changes nothing, because the cursor has already moved past that hour. Found on Camille Quesada’s 2026-08-06 19:32Z booking mail (ticket 067): Junyan’s reply reached canon, her question never did, so the thread reads as the desk writing to her unprompted. Every other turn across Aug 5–7 was present. This was not the outage — the cursor rides in the same commit as the messages, so an outage-lost message re-captures on the next tick. This one was skipped.

    The repair fixes the class, not the instance. Once every 24h the poller re-lists the last 7 days with the same query and writes whatever canon is missing. It heals any kind of skip — spam, an API blip, a mid-page failure, a delivery whose internalDate lands behind the cursor — not just the one that was found.

    includeSpamTrash deliberately stays false. This is the design decision worth remembering: mail still sitting in spam is spam and stays out of the ledger, and a human clicking “Not spam” is the repair — the next re-scan sees it and files it. Flipping the flag would have bought the same recovery plus a second exclusion list to keep correct forever.

    Three properties designed for, then tested. The re-scan never moves the forward cursor (it reads old mail by definition; letting it touch last_epoch would be a way to skip live mail — the defect, rebuilt). It is loud: a recovery prints ::warning:: with the count, because a re-scan that healed quietly would hide the fact that the forward pass has a blind spot. And it is nearly free — the existing one-tree-scan have set means an already-captured message costs a dict lookup and no messages.get call, so a week of re-listing is a handful of list calls and zero writes in steady state.

    ✓ Proved by running it, not by reasoning. A message three days old and far behind the cursor was deleted from canon; the forward pass correctly did not see it, the re-scan recovered it byte-identical and said so, and last_epoch was untouched. The 24h gate held on an immediate re-run; a forced re-scan in steady state wrote nothing. dc-canon 8e79f74e.

    Two things learned on the way, both worth keeping. A spam round-trip is label-destructive — marking a live message SPAM and removing SPAM stripped IMPORTANT and CATEGORY_*, leaving only user labels — so labels can never serve as evidence of a message’s spam history; the trace is erased by the rescue itself. And the business Gmail connector can trash but cannot untrash (untrash_message → “The caller does not have permission”); removing the TRASH label directly works.

    Why this one is worse than it looks. Meanwhile create_user was unaffected the entire time, so the one operation that compounds the error kept working while the one that prevents it was dead.     The Actions budget was a $0 hard cap, not a failed card — and the workflow’s own cost comment was fictionFIX

    “Recent account payments have failed or your spending limit needs to be increased” reads like a dead credit card. It was not. All five account budgets sit at $0 with Stop usage: Yes — a hard cap that halts billable Actions the moment the free tier is gone. Only desk-correspondence notices, because it is the fleet’s only private repo with workflows and therefore the only one drawing paid minutes; fbs-monitor, day-sheet and night-watch are public, run on free minutes, and stayed green throughout — which is precisely why the repo looked alive while its jobs were being refused.

    The cadence in the file was fiction. watchlist.yml claimed “every 30 min ≈ 30 runs/day ≈ 900 min/month, comfortably inside the free tier” since 08-18. Counted from the API: cron-job.org fires it every 15 minutes, 60 runs/day — the intent to halve the tick never reached the ticker, and nothing in git could have contradicted it, because watchlist.yml carries only workflow_dispatch and cadence is not readable from the repo at all. Measured: watchlist ~1,824 + qa-review ~730 = ~2,554 min/month against a 2,000 quota (128%).

    The counter-intuitive bit, worth remembering: GitHub rounds every job UP to a whole minute, and the medians are 56s and 9s — so both bill a full minute and run count is the only lever. Speeding a run up saves exactly zero. A 9-second qa-review costs the same as a 55-second one.

    Junyan’s call: keep 15-min freshness, raise the budget — ~554 overage min × $0.008/min ≈ $4.43/mo, which is cheaper than the freshness it buys. No cadence or ticker change was made. The wrong arithmetic is corrected in the workflow header (1ac4dea4), and RUNTIMES.md gained the ticker row it never had — that missing row is how the drift went unseen for two days.

  • 2026-08-20 · 01:34Z

    The Registrar is live — direct bookings now set themselves up every 30 minutesNEW

    Nothing owned direct bookings. Skedda self-bookings, platform instant-books and request-accepts piled up unread under Sales - Skedda — 37 deep when it was charted — and the backlog sweep that cleared them found paid bookings with no FBS row at all. Ruled in wayfinder concierge-repair 079, built in 081, shipped in 082. Named The Registrar: it writes the records before a renter arrives, and the Doorman does the letting-in.

    Route: Cloud Scheduler job booking-sweep (us-central1) → POST event-gate /booking-sweep/<token>, */30 * * * * UTC, 24/7. Created and ENABLED by Junyan 01:34Z on revision event-gate-00047-rvm; first fire 02:00Z. Deterministic code, no LLM in the hot path — every judgment call raises an ✅ Actions residue row instead of guessing, and a human reads the residue.

    The shape that matters: there is no processed-set. Each pass re-derives from the owning surface what should exist by now — Artist row, a working alarm code, an FBS board row at T−2, a queued confirmation — and creates only what is missing. Stateless and idempotent: a missed pass costs nothing, a double pass writes nothing twice. Mail is not consumed at all — the read/unread bit on that label is a known-corrupted signal (078: messages marked read contained genuinely unprocessed bookings), so the lane reads the Skedda board directly and mail is pure echo.

    Seams. Defers entirely to send-engine/SameDay.gs for bookings starting today or tomorrow — it checks but never writes inside that window. Tier messages stay with the 04:00 Host batch; this lane only guarantees prerequisites. It never sends and never touches money: confirmations land at Pending Review.

    Stopping it: pause the scheduler job (fastest, reversible). BOOKING_SWEEP_DISABLED=1 also works but is not currently set — it defaults to off, which is why the lane went live the moment the job existed. Use --update-env-vars, never --set-env-vars: the live service carries 15 variables and the second form drops the rest.

    Verified before release: live dry pass 165 scanned · 125 skipped · 10 deferred · 0 residue · 0 errors. The same pass on the previous revision filed one residue row that ticket 085’s id-first lookup resolved — the clearest evidence yet that the name join was the defect, not the renter. Suite 397.

  • 2026-08-20 · 01:30Z

    The Skedda anti-forgery token was outliving its own session, and every read went anonymousFIX

    The token is minted FOR a session, but the cache was a bare process global. When the cookie rotated underneath it, a warm Cloud Run instance kept presenting the old token — and Skedda answered HTTP 200 with the holders stripped. Not an error, not a 401: real-looking bookings with price 0, payment_status 0, user null, title null. Nothing raised, so nothing noticed. This is the same anonymous-read signature that cost the fleet a day on 08-11, arriving by a different door.

    Fixed in services/event-gate/skedda_read.py: the cache is keyed on a sha256 fingerprint of the cookie that produced the token, so a rotation invalidates it automatically, and carries a 15-minute TTL (SKEDDA_TOKEN_TTL) so no token can outlive its session in a long-warm instance. reset_session_cache() drops the token and the space-name cache together; the 401/403 path calls it instead of half-clearing. The fingerprint is deliberately not the cookie — that value ends up in comparisons and, one careless log line later, in Cloud Logging.

    The stripped read now self-heals. The Registrar drops the cached session and reads once more before giving up, so a stale token repairs itself inside the same pass with no human involved. A second stripped read still fails loudly and files sweep-blind — the retry must not paper over a genuinely non-admin cookie, which is a different fault with a different fix. Six new tests: rotation forces a re-scrape, a stable cookie does not, TTL expiry, the retry, the retry not hiding a real failure, and the fingerprint never leaking the cookie. Suite 397.
