    A card's "booking today" scope was enforced only by the code lane — every LLM lane read it as adviceFIX

    Junyan flagged a queued row: an address reply to Hanano I. [Giggster] for a Studio 693 booking on Sat Aug 22 — eight days out. The matched How to Respond row, Which address / building, is scoped in its Auto-send Criteria to "a watchlist-verified renter with a booking today." The Responder matched it anyway, swapped "today" for the real booking date per RESPONSE-RESOLVER step 2, and queued at Pending Review.

    Nothing overrode a gate — the gate was never binding off the code path. In the event-gate lane the constraint is structural: cards.py filters on Send Mode = Auto-send and never reads Auto-send Criteria at all, but the watchlist it gates on is today's bookings, so a not-today renter cannot reach a card. The Responder has no watchlist, and the field's name says it governs auto-send. It declined to auto-send and held for review — by its own reading, correctly. The scope lived in prose, in a field named for a different decision, on lanes that were never told to enforce it.

    Fix (canon, dc-canon c436b06f): RESPONSE-RESOLVER step 3 gains context gate (d) — a row's Auto-send Criteria bounds the row's use, not merely its auto-send, on any lane at any Status. An unsatisfiable criteria line is a NO MATCH (go to step 4.5), and the mismatch may not be repaired by rewriting a variable: substituting a real future date for "today" widens a row's scope rather than satisfying it.

    Second defect, filed as an HTR suggestion (agents never edit HTR rows): this row's wording carries no sender identity. Invisible in the case it was written for — Eunyi C, mid-booking, inside a live SMS thread she started. Outside it, the renter gets a cold text from a number with zero prior history that knows her name, studio, date and time window. The phone.com desk line confirmed no SMS has ever been exchanged with hers.

    Follow-on, same day: Junyan answered her by hand in the Giggster app with "693 Bloor St West, Lower level (near Christie Station)" — a floor the card did not hold and could never have sent. Every other studio in STUDIO_ADDRESS already carried a floor or suite; 693 was the only bare street number. Fixed in cards.py (dc-canon 3feb7036) and in the Notion row's studio map together — and that pairing is the point: the addresses that actually send live in code, while the Notion row's map is duplicated documentation. Change one and the card sends one thing while the row claims another. Both now say so explicitly. Needs an event-gate deploy to take effect.

    Not a defect: SMS was the right channel — step 6 prefers it for US/CA numbers once a direct phone is on file, even for platform renters, and she volunteered the number in the Giggster thread when asked. Still open: the HTR row itself is unchanged pending Junyan's decision on both proposals, and the same "scope stated in prose the code doesn't read" shape exists on every other Auto-send card.

  • 2026-08-14 · 13:44Z

    First real bypass proves the watchdog — and proves nothing else was watchingVALIDATED

    Studio 901's front door was found bypassed while 901 sat armed_away. The building reads as armed on every board; the front door would not have triggered if opened. Junyan mentioned it in passing — which is exactly how this class surfaces today.

    It is the first live exercise of the bypass logic shipped 2026-08-13, and the decision was correct end to end: it matched the panel's real wording Sensor Bypassed (Studio 901 Front Door) — until now guessed at with a loose substring, because no bypass existed to read; resolved the sensor to exactly one partition by name; and proposed the right action (disarm 901 only, re-arm cleanly without force_bypass, restore armed_away). It ignored the four standing Credentials In Conflict troubles, as intended. Dry run, so nothing was sent.

    The part worth keeping: nothing in the fleet surfaces this. The Doorman's morning check names bypassed sensors and their Sensor Bypassed trouble mirror in its explicit do-not-file list, as expected daily noise. That was a reasonable call when nothing could act on them — it means a stuck bypass has no reporting path at all, and an armed studio with a switched-off front door looks identical to a healthy one. The watchdog's bypass pass exists precisely for this, and it took a real occurrence to demonstrate the gap rather than argue it.

    One flaw exposed by the same data: the watchdog's panel-level report says the trouble feed "does not say which sensor." It does — the name sits in the parentheses. Harmless today (that path is report-only, and the sensor-flag path caught it anyway) but it double-reports, and the stated reasoning is now falsified. Parse the name out and the panel-level report can name its target.

    Then the fix was attempted by hand — and the attempt taught the wrong lesson first. Two full disarm → arm-away cycles on 901 alone (force_bypass=false, each verified), and both re-reads still showed the sensor bypassed. Recorded at the time as "a clean re-arm does not clear a bypass on these panels." By 15:47Z the bypass was gone, along with its trouble condition, with 901 still armed_away.

    The reads were too fast to judge. Each cycle's verified=True polled the partition's armed state and nothing else; the bypass flag was read seconds after the re-arm returned. This account's most expensive standing rule is that an immediate read-back proves nothing — the panels sync after the call returns. So the cycles proved the partition armed and proved nothing about the bypass. Whether the second re-arm landed late, or someone dealt with the door in the two-hour gap, cannot be recovered after the fact.

    What survives the correction: Junyan's two-attempt cap, now built in as a per-sensor BypassLedger whose third pass emits a report-only bypass_stuck. A watchdog must not cycle a live partition indefinitely regardless of which explanation is true. What has to change: an attempt must not be counted failed on an immediate read. The bypass pass runs fifteen minutes apart, which is probably settle enough; the manual test ran back-to-back and was not, which is exactly how it produced a confident wrong conclusion.

    Still open: no hardware fault is established for 901 — the Maintenance row is resolved and nobody should be sent to that door on its strength. A recurrence is the signal worth acting on. Also unchanged: the in-memory trip ledger must become durable before anything is armed, since a cold start would reset a suppressed partition.

    Why no alert fired — the interesting part. The ⏱️ Send-Timing Verifier ran fine (09:23Z, 71s, success) and its timing audit was genuinely clean. It even found the missing Manuel AVA on its own. So a real finding left as a missable phone buzz and no durable artifact. Every component behaved as specified; the specification was scoped to "will this send at the wrong time?" when the failure was "will this send at all?"

Also recorded: Time to Send is not SQL-queryable and killed v1's first query on every run.

    Still open: why the Host regressed at all (no run report was emitted); a deterministic version of these checks in invariants.py, so an LLM is not the only auditor of an LLM; and an ops-lead review skill with a durable daily output for the judgment-shaped cases an invariant cannot see — like Manuel's duplicate text, where every field is individually correct.

  • 2026-08-13 · 23:55Z

    The invariants checker stops re-filing its own complaints — memory grace window + crash-safe state publishFIX

    Root cause of the ~95-row board found and closed at the source (commit f80ac103, on top of the board-truth dedupe in d4bec0a0). The checker's state dropped any defect absent for one pass — but Notion queries transiently omit rows, so a defect that "cleared" for a single read came back with runs=0, escalated=False and re-filed the same complaint on reaching the threshold. Absent defects now keep their run count and escalated flag for MISSING_DROP_AFTER=3 consecutive absent passes before being believed cleared.

    Second window closed in the same commit: publish() deleted the old report blocks before writing the new — a crash between the two left the 🩺 page with no state at all, a full cold start that reset every counter. It now writes first, deletes after; state chunks carry a generation stamp and parse_state() takes the newest generation that parses, falling back to the older instead of amnesia. 12 new/updated tests, 71 passing.

    VERIFIED LIVE (2026-08-15 01:50Z). The fix shipped in revision event-gate-00032-blr at 16:17Z Aug 14, earlier than assumed — the generation stamp in the 🩺 page's state blocks (DESK-INVARIANTS-STATE-V1 0/3 20260815T012505) exists only in this code, so it proves which build is serving. Nine hourly passes of evidence: 5 new checker rows filed, every one a genuinely new defect escalating at exactly its 3rd run, zero duplicates across the 12 open rows. Before the fix the board grew to ~95.

    The grace window was exercised for real. The 19:25Z pass died on RemoteDisconnected: Remote end closed connection without response — a Notion blip, not a code fault. It filed one Run Error row and a later clean pass marked it Processed. State survived intact: the oldest counter still reads runs: 143 with first_seen Aug 5, where a cold start would have reset every counter to 1 and re-filed the lot. That is the exact failure this commit was written for, caught in the wild within hours.

    No drain needed after all. The Custodian cancelled the backlog (94 Cancelled, 18 Processed); the 12 rows still open are distinct, real, and point at live stale tickets. Revision 00033-qbl (01:43Z) is a routine redeploy carrying the Doorman change; env block held at 14 variables, /health returns {"ok":true,"disabled":false}.

    The cookie refresh loop is finally closed — and the guard's own failure mode was the last defectFIX + PROOF

    The Aug 12–13 refresh aborts were not a bad cookie. The job runs python hosting/refresh_and_store.py, so sys.path[0] is hosting/ — import skedda failed and the admin guard failed closed on its own import bug, printing "cookie is not admin-authenticated" for a cookie it never tested. Worse than a no-op: because Skedda keeps one active session per user, each aborted run had already logged in and killed the fleet's live cookie on the way down. A broken refresh job is an active saboteur — a paused one is safer than a failing one.

    Fix 4c56f86 (repo root onto sys.path), image rebuilt 21:21Z, scheduler paused, then a supervised manual run (skedda-refresh-gl22s) proved every link: login → _cookie_is_admin probe passed → cookie stored → skedda-mcp bounced (COOKIE_REFRESHED_AT=2026-08-13T21:26:30Z) → connector read-back returned real prices and holders. Scheduler re-enabled by Junyan; the 06:00 Toronto daily run now deliberately spends and replaces the key each morning. RUNTIMES.md row flipped from 🔴 to ✅ the same hour.

    Still open, filed as a Sweep Feedback design row: an aborted refresh alerts nobody (exit 1 into a log nobody reads — three consecutive aborts were found only by a human during a fixer walk), and the one-session rule means any human login to the same Skedda account kills the fleet's cookie — a dedicated robot account would end that race.

    The Night Watch measures the real backlog now — and a third of the Ops Lead queue was the invariants checker talking to itselfCONTRACT + CLEANUP

    The console rendered 1 needs you on a morning with 139 open Action rows and 48 open tickets, the oldest 40 days. It was obeying its contract exactly: render only the residuals JOB 34 names. That contract assumed a Concierge that keeps up, and the Concierge now skips half its jobs for budget most passes — so the backlog was invisible and the Ops Lead was hunting two boards by hand.

    Two additions to junyanboon/night-watch, both read straight from the data sources and both independent of the marker: a truth band (open Actions by owner, open tickets per board, age of the oldest — always rendered, including on a failed handshake) and ACT II “The queue” (open tickets grouped by Position, Gate: Staff first, then Review, then by age). Both are gauges and pointers — process-studio-tickets still owns every ticket mechanic. The publisher picked them up on its own next run with no trigger change.

    Two publisher defects the first live run exposed, now gated: it read pass 1's marker when three later passes had superseded it, publishing five residuals a 10:35 pass had already closed — so the rule is now take the last marker, and no residual reaches ACT I without its own live row read in this run (the marker nominates, only the row admits). And it flagged the retired Opener as a missing report; the retirement moved out of a Sources bullet into a named Retired runs table plus a validate-step check.

    Then the queue itself: 99 → 62 — and then straight back up, because the cleanup was wrong. Thirty-five rows were Raised by = Desk Invariants: persistent-defect notices about other tickets (“untouched 50h”, “gate with no action row”). They were cancelled on the reasoning that they duplicated each other and that ACT II now shows the same tickets once, with their age. Within hours the checker had refiled eight of them.

    ⚠️ The correction, and it is the useful part of this entry. The checker does not refile every run — invariants.py:already_escalated_ids() reads its own open rows and skips any defect whose subject page is already named, so one open row per subject suppresses all further filing. Cancelling 35 rows while their underlying conditions were still true emptied that dedupe set; the defects were still above the 3-run threshold, so it refiled at up to ESCALATE_MAX_PER_RUN = 5 a run. The duplicates that motivated the sweep (one sales ticket with three rows) were legacy: board-dedupe landed 2026-08-10 after Custodian D2, and every duplicate pair predates it. The generator had been fixed four days earlier.

    The rule this establishes: a persistent-defect row is cleared by fixing the condition it names — advance the ticket, set Revisit After, raise the missing gate row — never by cancelling the notice. Cancel only when the condition is genuinely gone or the subject ticket is closed; the checker then retires it on its own after MISSING_DROP_AFTER = 3 absent passes. Cancelling a live notice is not a cleanup, it is a snooze that costs a re-file. (Five of the 35 did point at already-closed tickets and were correctly cancelled.)

    Related canon fix the same day: JOB 34's needs_ops must equal the number of RESIDUAL lines, never checked − completed — a miscount that had been forcing “handoff incomplete” and suppressing the list entirely.

    Three lane-specific carve-outs were written in rather than papered over, because a rule that is wrong in a lane gets ignored in every lane. Platform Instant Book scopes the rule to its fallback path only: the default is an in-thread Gmail reply with the queue row set to Will Not Send, where threading fields are moot, but the documented fallback (staff add the relay to the Artist Page and release the row) does dispatch for real. That bullet now carries an explicit exception for what a step writes, and a new §4 principle requires every queue-write step to name its own fields, on every queue step rather than the first. Mirrored as a short cross-reference in capture-sop-update. Without this, each new SOP written from the templates would have reproduced the gap indefinitely.

    Two things surfaced were closed the same day with Junyan's go-ahead. Platform — Last Minute Booking Request (legacy) now carries the deprecation banner pointing at the AI-Ready page — the 🗺️ SOP Routing DB was checked first and no row pointed at it, so no re-pointing was needed. And the silent SQL omission was reproduced before recording: the identical query over the SOPs data source returned 14 rows in one run and 16 minutes later — transient staleness, not a filter bug. Now a Known-environment gotcha in the workspace CLAUDE.md: a single query-data-sources result is never proof of coverage; cross-check against a second signal before claiming completeness.

    The evidence existed all along, one hop upstream: the TELUS/ADT notification emails (Gmail label Artist Care - ADT) name the person right in the subject — the same stream the FBS board and the Timekeeper already parse. Doorman prompt is at v3 with the Gmail connector attached (read-only, that label only): Job C now searches the label per candidate. Attribution taxonomy verified against the live mailbox: by <Name> = keypad presence; (info@danceannex.ca) = Junyan's remote disarms, never renter presence; (Panel User) = unattributed keypad entry (rare, ~3 in 14 days).

    Two contingencies baked in: a feed canary — zero label mail in 48h across five studios is never real, so the sweep suspends itself and raises a Review row instead of reading silence as absence; and a keep-bias guard — an unattributed (Panel User) disarm counts as presence for anyone booked in the window, because a wrongly kept code is clutter but a wrongly deleted one is a lockout. Canon doorman-access-lifecycle.md updated to match, pushed to dc-canon.

  • 2026-08-13 · 02:55Z

    The send engine now refuses an unthreaded reply instead of quietly sending oneGUARD

    A Message Queue row whose Email Subject starts Re: but whose Source Email is blank used to take the brand-new-email path and succeed — the row read Sent, and the renter got a "Re: …" attached to nothing she wrote, splitting her conversation into two threads. Nothing in the outcome distinguished that from a correct reply. Engine v18 checks the pair in validate_(): reply-shaped plus blank field now flips the row to Error with a row comment and an alert email. Live-proven the same night — test row errored ~45s after release, alert quoted the guard's own text, recipient inbox stayed empty (matrix case M10).

    This is Desk Invariant J, which canon had required in prose since 2026-07-09 — and which still lost 7 reply-shaped rows as new threads between Jul 25 and Aug 4, including three on the Anneka K. Peerspace relay. A sent row cannot be repaired, so the check moved to the boundary. The invariant checker still flags J at Pending Review hours earlier; the two surfaces are deliberately different — catch-early versus refuse-late.

    The instructive part is why the field was blank. Canon mandated it in four places, every one of them in the Responder / RESPONSE-RESOLVER / studio-ticket-triage lanes. The row that surfaced this (a time-slot enquiry, caught pre-send) was drafted by the Concierge straight from a Notion SOP whose queue-write step said only "Channel per lead source, held at Pending Review". The rule was written down nowhere the agent was reading at write time. Fixed in three places: that SOP names the field, concierge-orchestration.md binds it to every queue-writing path including SOP steps, and the engine no longer trusts any of them.

  • 2026-08-13 · 02:30Z

    Alarm.com windows retired — access is now presence-based, and the Doorman owns the lifecyclePOLICY + REROUTE

    Renter codes are now provisioned with no window — a code exists on the panels or it doesn't. Windows earned retirement twice in one day: a freshly written window silently reverted within an hour of a 200 response (Kate Keenan, user 247495111 — a released HTA had already told her she was set), and an expired window wedges its code until a delete, ~10-minute panel sync, and re-add.

    Canon: library/doorman-access-lifecycle.md in dc-canon. Doorman cloud prompt is at v2 with three jobs: the access pre-flight widened from today+tomorrow to 7 days; a revert check re-reads any Access/PIN row marked Processed in the last 48h against the live panel; and a retirement sweep — idle 30 days plus no bookings 60 days ahead — raises Human-confirmed delete rows, max 5 per pass, protected list (staff, allow_permanent, active memberships) untouchable. The sweep never deletes; staff do, in the UI.

    The delete/re-add race that broke Kate's morning is also now a canon rule: never delete-then-readd to change access — the gap invites a second writer, and the late re-add clobbers whatever landed in between. Exactly that happened between 08:50 and 09:44 on Aug 12.

  • 2026-08-13 · 02:30Z

    The Doorman's 04:00 arm-check is backFIX

    Cron restored to 0 8,10 UTC with Junyan's approval — the live trigger had silently been single-fire (0 10) for an unknown stretch, so the overnight "are the empty studios armed" pass simply wasn't happening. Verified on a live re-read; next 04:00 fire is tonight. Closes the defect logged below.

  • 2026-08-13

    skedda-mcp learns user tags; refresh job learns to distrust its own cookieNEW TOOLS + GUARD

    Two new connector tools, list_user_tags and set_user_tags (dry-run unless confirmed, replaces the whole tag set like the admin UI does), and find_user now returns each person's tags. This closes the Studio Viewing loop: the tag that caps a renter at 15 minutes and $0 is finally visible and removable through the MCP instead of only in the admin UI.

    The nightly cookie refresh gained _cookie_is_admin() — it probes an admin-only endpoint and aborts rather than storing a cookie that can't pass. Weeks of "successful" refreshes had been storing anonymous cookies; because the venue schedule is public, anonymous reads succeed with every price at 0 and every holder null. Silently wrong money data, not a visible failure. Until a verified admin cookie is seeded, treat every fleet Skedda read as suspect — the Doorman's retirement sweep explicitly aborts if bookings come back holderless.

  • 2026-08-13 · 02:20–02:30Z

    alarm-watchdog goes live in dry runNEW ROUTE

    Two Cloud Scheduler jobs now tick the new /watchdog route on alarm-mcp: every minute for alarms, every fifteen for bypasses. Deterministic — no tokens, so it keeps watching through an outage.

    It decides and logs; it cannot act. WATCHDOG_ARMED is unset, and the executor that would carry an action out does not exist yet — the route returns 501 even if the variable is set. Two locks, deliberately.

    Purpose once armed: let a tripped siren run 90 seconds, disarm that partition only, re-arm to the mode it was found in. Ceiling of two trips per partition per hour, then it stops and calls a human instead — repeated trips are exactly when a robot should stop silencing an alarm.

  • 2026-08-13 · 02:09Z

    One shared Alarm.com session replaces login-per-callRESILIENCE

    Every panel read used to perform a full login and close it: 14.8 seconds, measured. At one-minute cadence that is ~1,440 logins a day against a provider that locks accounts for repeated authentication — and the token died twice on 2026-08-12 while several surfaces were logging in independently.

    Now: one session, reused. Warm calls run 0.83s. Re-login happens only on session death or after six hours, and exactly once — never in a loop, because a lockout costs the whole system while a missed tick costs sixty seconds. Non-session errors never trigger a retry, so a write that failed mid-flight is never blindly re-run.

    Everything downstream of alarm-mcp gets faster for free, including the presence gate's /panel-states call.

  • 2026-08-13

    The panel can now say "the siren is sounding"SIGNAL

    Partitions previously reported one flag, faulted, which was true for a low battery, a propped door, a broken sensor and an active alarm alike. Right question for the morning check; useless for anything that acts. Automation keyed to it would have disarmed the studios over a dying battery.

    in_alarm is now its own field, meaning only that the siren is going off. faulted is unchanged, so nothing downstream shifted.

    Still unproven: nothing has tripped since, so in_alarm has never been observed true. A deliberate trip on 693 is pending — that single test decides whether the whole watchdog design holds.

  • 2026-08-13

    The Doorman's overnight arm-check has not been firingDEFECT

    The registry claimed a twice-daily trigger (0 8,10 UTC), branching on the hour: 04:00 for the overnight arm-check that inherited the retired Closer's job, ~06:00 for the access preflight. The live trigger reads 0 10 — a single fire.

    So the morning preflight has been running and the 04:00 "are the empty studios armed" pass has not, for an unknown stretch. Restoring it is a deliberate decision, not a silent fix. (Resolved same night — see the FIX entry above.)

  • 2026-08-13

    alarm-mcp is no longer read-only, and its runbook now says soDOCS

    Panel control landed 2026-08-11 — arm_alarm, disarm_alarm, panel_state — but README-gcp.md still promised "read-only by construction, the package has no arm/disarm/lock calls." Anyone judging how much to trust the service got a wrong answer from its own documentation.

    Corrected, along with a retracted theory in the provision route that blamed vanishing access windows on timestamp seconds. Both now state plainly that they were wrong, rather than quietly reading correctly — the failure worth remembering is that the docs kept vouching for a guarantee the code had dropped.

    Connectors cache their tool lists. A redeploy alone will not surface a new tool; the connector has to reconnect.

  • 2026-08-11

    Map compiledORIGIN

    First pass, built from RUNTIMES.md and the event-gate source in dc-canon. Established the central question the map exists to answer: which paths survive a Claude token outage, and which stop dead.

Open, as of 2026-08-13

  • ✅ Resolved 2026-08-13: admin cookie seeded and the refresh loop proven live — see the FIX + PROOF entry above. Remaining follow-ups moved to a Sweep Feedback design row: loud alert on refresh failure, dedicated robot Skedda account.
  • Migrate legacy alarm windows. Existing windowed users surface one at a time in the Doorman's 7-day table as legacy-window; each migration is a manual delete → 10-min sync → re-add-as-permanent. No big-bang panel edit.
  • Trip 693 on purpose. Nothing else proves in_alarm fires on a real trip, or that disarming clears it.
  • Bypass on a disarmed studio — hold and report, or arm it to clear? Clearing means arming, and a disarmed studio may have someone inside.
  • Rotate mcp-auth-token. It reached a chat transcript. Rotation now has three touchpoints: the secret, the alarm-mcp redeploy, and both scheduler job headers — miss one and every tick 401s silently.
  • Durable trip ledger before the watchdog is armed. The count lives in memory today; a cold start would reset a suppressed partition and let it auto-clear again.
  • The email emergency gap — unchanged since the map was compiled.
Kill switches: GATE_DISABLED (emergency SMS lane) · LOOP_DISABLED · PRESENCE_GATE_DISABLED · INVARIANTS_DISABLED · SAMEDAY_ENABLED (ships off) · WATCHDOG_ARMED (unset = dry run). Scheduler trust: Cloud Scheduler + RemoteTrigger + Apps Script reliable; GitHub Actions cron unreliable fleet-wide.
